| I | An internal event reaches a browser. `UserSessionsRevoked` (03a) is a global event with no swimlane and names a user, so a per-user filter alone would stream it, with that user's id, to every Viewer. | The SSE edge drops every event for which `DomainEvent::is_internal()` is true before any per-user filter, on live delivery and on replay (one `deliverable` check for both); the default visibility rule refuses such an event to every role as well, so it is not the hole behind the edge. A request cannot name an internal kind: `kinds=user_sessions_revoked` is a 400, as any name outside the OpenAPI list is, and the kind is refused whole and never dropped from the list, because an emptied list would mean every kind. The bus still carries internal events to in-process subscribers on every replica, which is what 03a's session cache needs. | `an_internal_event_is_never_streamed_live_to_a_viewer_or_an_admin`, `an_internal_event_is_never_replayed_to_a_viewer_or_an_admin`, `an_internal_event_that_is_both_replayed_and_live_is_still_dropped`, `a_resync_and_the_other_kinds_still_reach_the_users_they_reached_before`, `the_default_visibility_rule_refuses_an_internal_event_to_every_role`, `an_internal_kind_cannot_be_asked_for_and_agrees_with_the_event`, `sse_query_bounds_are_enforced`, `an_internal_event_crosses_the_replicas_on_the_bus_and_reaches_no_browser`, `an_internal_event_in_the_outbox_is_not_replayed_to_a_resuming_browser` | S4, S12 |
| I | The CA key is exported, or a code path signs with a key that is not in KMS. | Issuance takes a `KmsSigner`, a key name and the CA certificate and nothing else; every certificate signature is a `sign_digest` call over the certificate's own to-be-signed bytes; a source scan fails on any private-key constructor or CA key name outside the one leaf-key function; at start-up the CA certificate must match the key KMS signs with. | `ca_key_is_only_used_through_kms_signer_source_scan`, `the_scan_fails_on_a_planted_private_key_constructor`, `the_scan_fails_on_a_planted_ca_key_name_and_on_a_secret_source_in_the_ca_module`, `ca_issuer_new_takes_only_a_signer_a_key_name_and_a_certificate`, `every_certificate_signature_came_from_sign_digest_over_its_own_tbs`, `a_ca_certificate_that_does_not_match_the_kms_key_is_refused_at_start_up`, `a_kms_failure_means_no_certificate` | S5, S7 |
| I | A KMS-wrapped secret is decrypted for the wrong owner. | Envelope wrapping binds the associated data, so a ciphertext does not decrypt under another user or credential id. | `kms_envelope_wrap_binds_aad`, `kms_envelope_conformance` | S7 |
| I | Traffic between components is readable or downgradable. | TLS 1.3 only on the web, agent and replica listeners and on the Google clients, no session resumption on the agent listener, and `verify-full` to the database. | `web_listener_is_tls13_only`, `a_tls_config_supports_exactly_tls13`, `agent_listener_refuses_tls12`, `agent_listener_negotiates_tls13_and_h2_and_does_not_resume`, `google_http_refuses_tls12_wrong_roots_and_a_certificate_for_another_host` | S6 |
| I | The operations listener discloses internals. | It answers GET only, on a cluster-internal port; `/healthz` names nothing, `/readyz` names the failing check and no cause, `/metrics` labels are route templates and status classes, never a path, user or id. | `healthz_is_ok_and_names_nothing`, `readyz_follows_the_checks_and_names_the_failing_one`, `readyz_names_the_mirrors_while_they_are_empty`, `metrics_are_labelled_by_route_template_and_never_by_path_user_or_id`, `the_operations_listener_answers_get_only_and_nothing_else` | S10, S16 |
| D | An oversized or deeply nested body, message or event exhausts memory. | Bodies are capped (4 MiB at the edge, 1 MiB for the webhook, whose declared length is refused before the secret is read); chunked bodies without a length are capped too; JSON nesting is limited to 64; gRPC messages are size-limited and a declared size is refused before the payload arrives; an event over 4 KiB becomes a resync; a blob over 4 MiB is refused. | `oversized_body_is_rejected_413_before_handler`, `a_body_exactly_at_the_cap_is_accepted`, `json_nesting_depth_is_limited`, `oversized_webhook_body_is_413_before_the_secret_is_even_read`, `a_body_over_the_hub_cap_is_refused_by_the_limits_layer_and_one_over_the_webhook_cap_by_the_handler`, `proto_decode_refuses_a_declared_size_before_the_payload_arrives`, `replica_forward_refuses_a_message_larger_than_the_limit_before_decoding_it`, `an_event_over_the_size_cap_is_stored_as_a_resync` | S11 |
| D | A flood of requests, or a spoofed `X-Forwarded-For`, exhausts the hub or hides an abuser. | A per-IP bucket (cheapest, first), a per-user bucket after the user is known, both in moka caches with a time-to-idle and a maximum size so that a flood of new addresses evicts old buckets; the client address is the entry counted from the right by the configured number of proxies; a slow request gets a 408. | `per_ip_limit_returns_429_with_retry_after_and_refills`, `per_user_limit_follows_the_user_across_addresses`, `a_spoofed_forwarded_for_does_not_change_the_bucket`, `the_bucket_tables_are_bounded`, `a_request_that_takes_too_long_gets_a_408_problem` | S11, S21 |
| D | Slow or idle connections hold the listeners, a handshake never finishes, or a client reads a stream too slowly. | A semaphore bounds open connections and a connection over the limit is closed at once; the TLS handshake and the request head have deadlines; an idle agent stream is closed; an SSE client that stops reading is cut off after a write timeout. | `connections_over_the_limit_are_closed_and_slots_are_reused`, `a_connection_that_never_finishes_its_request_head_is_closed`, `a_connection_that_never_finishes_its_handshake_is_closed`, `connections_over_the_limit_are_closed_without_an_answer`, `connect_closes_an_idle_stream`, `a_client_that_stops_reading_is_cut_off_after_the_write_timeout` | S11, S21 |
| D | A flood of webhook deliveries or join attempts causes unbounded work. | Pushes to one repository coalesce into one fetch job in a bounded queue, a full queue drops new targets and counts them (the 60 s poll catches the change), and every push is still acknowledged; join attempts are limited per swimlane and per address in bounded memory. | `webhook_flood_of_pushes_is_coalesced_and_bounded_and_every_push_is_still_acknowledged`, `pushes_to_one_repo_coalesce_into_one_fetch_job`, `a_full_queue_drops_new_targets_and_counts_them`, `join_attempts_are_limited_per_swimlane_and_per_address` | S11, S18 |
| D | One agent, one slow stream or one forwarded request ties up the hub. | Each agent stream has a bounded outbound queue (64) and at most 16 requests in flight, also when they are forwarded from another replica; every request has a deadline; the pending table and the stream table are bounded. | `the_outbound_queue_holds_64_messages_and_then_says_full`, `at_most_sixteen_requests_are_in_flight_and_a_finished_one_frees_its_slot`, `the_in_flight_cap_of_an_agent_holds_for_forwarded_requests_too`, `the_table_is_bounded`, `the_table_of_streams_is_bounded`, `a_forwarded_request_times_out_at_its_own_deadline`, `a_request_the_agent_cannot_read_is_busy_not_blocked` | S11, S21 |
| D | The event bus or its subscribers grow without bound, or one slow subscriber blocks the others. | Each subscriber has a bounded queue; a full queue drops that subscriber's backlog and delivers a `Resync` first; a user holds at most 5 streams and a replica 2,000; a stream ends at its maximum age; a replay larger than its limit is a resync; the query is bounded to 64 swimlanes and 16 kinds. A batch is at most 200 events of at most 4 KiB each, written by one statement, and a bigger one is refused before the database is touched. | `slow_consumer_gets_resync_instead_of_blocking_the_bus`, `an_empty_oversized_or_foreign_batch_writes_nothing`, `a_batch_is_one_insert`, `connections_are_capped_per_user_and_in_total`, `a_stream_that_has_lived_its_maximum_age_is_closed_for_the_client_to_resume`, `a_replay_larger_than_the_limit_is_a_resync`, `sse_query_bounds_are_enforced` | S11 |
| D | The outbox table grows without bound, or its clean-up blocks the event path. | A leased job (one replica at a time) deletes rows older than one hour, oldest first, at most 1,000 rows per statement and 100 statements per run, checking its lease before each statement; the retention cannot be set below ten minutes, which is longer than the replay window and the relay's wait for a late commit together, so no row a reader may still ask for is deleted; a client whose last id was pruned is told to refetch. | `outbox_prune_deletes_rows_older_than_1h_in_batches`, `outbox_prune_does_at_most_its_batch_limit_per_run_and_the_next_run_goes_on`, `outbox_prune_stops_when_the_lease_is_lost`, `outbox_prune_refuses_a_retention_the_replay_window_could_outlive`, `outbox_prune_leaves_the_live_relay_and_replay_working`, `the_prune_job_runs_under_the_leased_runner_on_one_replica` | S11 |
| D | The caches grow past their budget: blobs, tree indexes, secrets. | Each cache is bounded by weight (bytes, or entries) and not by count; a blob larger than the whole cache is not kept; a stampede for one cold blob reads the database once; a batch lookup is capped; the secret cache is bounded and expires. | `blob_cache_is_bounded_by_weight`, `a_blob_bigger_than_the_whole_cache_is_not_kept`, `blob_cache_and_tree_cache_respect_their_caps`, `tree_index_cache_is_bounded_by_the_entries_it_holds_not_by_how_many_commits`, `a_stampede_for_one_cold_blob_reads_the_database_once`, `get_many_with_nothing_to_ask_or_too_much_never_reaches_the_database`, `secret_cache_expires_after_5_minutes_and_is_bounded` | S11, S21 |
| D | A dead or slow dependency (Google, Postgres, GitHub) stalls the hub. | Every external call has a timeout and bounded retries with full jitter; a query has a timeout; a fetch has a deadline and a failed one is retried a bounded number of times and then left to the poll; a readiness check that hangs makes the hub not ready after two seconds. | `kms_calls_retry_with_jitter_and_time_out`, `google_http_gives_up_on_a_server_that_never_answers`, `a_slow_database_is_cut_off_by_the_query_timeout`, `mirror_fetch_gives_up_at_its_deadline`, `the_worker_retries_a_failed_fetch_a_bounded_number_of_times_with_jitter_and_then_leaves_it_to_the_poll`, `a_check_that_hangs_makes_the_hub_not_ready_after_two_seconds`, `secret_manager_failures_are_unavailable_and_retried_a_bounded_number_of_times` | S21 |
| D | A replica dies, a stream closes or the hub shuts down, and requests hang. | Closing a stream completes every request in flight with an error; a request to a replica that is gone fails as unavailable; readiness fails first on shutdown, then streams get a reason to reconnect elsewhere; a lease is taken over after expiry. | `closing_the_stream_releases_the_registration_and_fails_requests_in_flight`, `a_request_to_a_replica_that_is_gone_fails_unavailable_without_hanging`, `three_hubs_agent_moves_during_traffic_no_request_lost`, `connect_closes_every_stream_when_the_hub_is_closing`, `shutdown_closes_every_stream_with_a_retry_hint`, `readiness_fails_as_soon_as_shutdown_begins_while_health_stays_ok`, `lease_taken_over_after_holder_dies` | S21 |
| E | A route is added without a guard, or one is reachable around its guard through another method. | Routes can only be registered through `GuardedRouter`, with a role or a stated reason for being public; a duplicate route or a public one without a reason is a build error; the route table is checked against `api/openapi.yaml`, and the platform declares exactly the three routes it owns. | `platform_route_table_is_guarded_and_matches_openapi`, `the_platform_declares_exactly_the_three_routes_it_owns`, `the_harness_rejects_a_platform_route_that_is_unguarded_or_guarded_too_low`, `the_route_table_lists_every_mounted_route_with_its_guard`, `a_public_route_without_a_reason_and_a_bad_path_are_build_errors`, `the_guard_is_not_reachable_around_through_another_method` | S4 |
| E | A stream outlives the user's right to it: the session ends, the user is disabled or the role drops. | The stream re-checks `SessionAuth::still_valid` on each heartbeat and ends within one heartbeat (15 s), and at its maximum age (30 minutes) so that a narrowing of visibility takes effect on reconnect. | `the_stream_ends_within_one_heartbeat_when_the_session_ends`, `the_stream_ends_when_the_role_drops_below_viewer`, `a_stream_over_a_real_socket_ends_when_the_user_is_disabled`, `a_stream_that_has_lived_its_maximum_age_is_closed_for_the_client_to_resume` | S4 |
| E | Served bytes run as script in the user's browser: a blob, a file under `web/dist`, an error page. | A blob is always `application/octet-stream` with `nosniff` and `attachment`; the HTML gets a CSP with a fresh nonce per response, no `unsafe-inline`, `unsafe-eval` or third-party origin, Trusted Types, COOP, `frame-ancestors 'none'` and HSTS; every route class has the full header set; the static handler is our own, refuses traversal in every spelling and never follows a symlink. | `headers_on_every_route_class`, `csp_nonce_is_fresh_per_response_and_matches_the_html`, `csp_has_no_unsafe_inline_unsafe_eval_or_third_party_origin`, `blob_is_never_rendered_inline`, `blob_is_served_as_a_download_never_inline`, `index_carries_the_nonce_of_its_own_response_on_every_tag`, `path_traversal_in_every_spelling_is_a_404`, `a_symlink_inside_the_web_root_is_never_followed`, `immutable_is_only_allowed_on_hashed_assets_and_blobs` | S12 |
| E | A test double reaches production: a fake session store lets anyone in, a policy that denies nothing serves withheld blobs. | The `fakes` and `conformance` features are enabled by dev-dependencies only and are not defaults; `DenyHashes` and the other doubles are not compiled into a release build; the binary refuses to start without the real identity module and registry instead of falling back to a fake. | `fakes_are_only_enabled_by_dev_dependencies`, `the_planted_manifest_is_caught` | S4, S21 |
| E | A parser flaw in untrusted input (an `AgentMessage`, a `SentinelMessage`, a forwarded request, a CSR, a webhook payload) crashes the hub or worse. | No unsafe code and no panics outside tests (clippy-denied), typed values at the boundary, and fuzz targets with committed seed corpora that `cargo test` replays on stable. | `hub_agent_message`, `hub_join_csr`, `hub_forward_request`, `hub_webhook_payload`, `hub_sentinel_message`, `corpus_replay_hub_agent_message`, `corpus_replay_hub_join_csr`, `corpus_replay_hub_forward_request`, `corpus_replay_hub_webhook_payload`, `corpus_replay_hub_sentinel_message`, `parse_csr_never_panics_and_only_accepts_p256_with_a_valid_signature`, `forward_request_decoding_never_panics_and_accepts_only_well_formed_calls`, `decode_never_panics_and_never_accepts_more_than_the_cap` | S21, S22 |
| E | A flaw in the hub gives an attacker a shell or a way to reach other systems. | Distroless digest-pinned image, non-root numeric user, read-only root, no capabilities, RuntimeDefault seccomp, no service-account token mounted, writable volumes limited to the mirror volume and an `emptyDir` for `/tmp`; the NetworkPolicy contract denies by default and lists only the egress destinations S18 names. | `hub_dockerfile_is_hardened`, `hardening_md_pod_contract_is_complete`, `hardening_md_network_policy_denies_by_default_and_lists_only_allowed_egress`, `hardening_lint_rejects_planted_violations` | S16, S18 |

### Residual risks

- **The blob endpoint depends on a policy that 05 supplies, and a hash is a capability.** `GET /api/v1/blobs/{hash}` asks a `BlobPolicy` (an `async` trait: the registry's answer needs its own queries) before it reads the store. The only implementation in this repository is the test double `DenyHashes`, which is not compiled into a release build, and the binary refuses to start without the real identity module and registry, so until 05 lands no deployed hub serves a blob. Once it does, "denied or withheld" is exactly as complete as 05's decision (D79). Roles are global (D35), not per swimlane, so any active user at Viewer or above who knows the hash of a blob that is not withheld may fetch it whichever swimlane it came from. The policy call is awaited inside the request, so the 30 s request timeout bounds a slow policy, and a policy that errors must fail closed (05's to prove).
- **A compromised app role can move `touched_at`, and the refresh is not audited.** Migration 0008 gives `lanekeeper_app` UPDATE on one column of `blobs`, `touched_at`, and on nothing else of a blob. A role that is compromised can set `touched_at` to any time, which delays or hastens the collection of any blob. It could already `DELETE` every blob (the grant is SELECT, INSERT and DELETE), so this is no new capability, and what it can do stays bounded: it cannot change a blob's bytes, address or size, and a blob that a `blob_refs`, `proposals` or `drafts` row names cannot be deleted at all (the foreign keys are RESTRICT). The refresh is not audited, like a blob insert and the planned collector deletes: a per-put audit event would put a write and an audit append on the ingest path of P15 for a row of operational state, and the content-addressed bytes it protects are not secret or changed by it.
- **The refresh protects what is stored by `put`, for the grace period, and nothing else.** A reference taken by an existence check without a `put` (the registry's `sync/record.rs`, `adoption/confirm.rs` and the guarded insert in `baselines.rs`) is not protected by it: only a `blob_refs` row is, and `baselines.blob_hash`, `file_state.nfs_hash` and `pr_links.nfs_hash` have no foreign key, so the retention's reference check (05) must read all of those columns. A blob that is referenced more than 24 h after it was stored (the staging lifetime that `MIN_RETENTION_GRACE` allows for, which the registry must range-check, and for example a prompt-06 upload preview) must have its row created when it is stored, or be put again when it is referenced. Both limits are written in `docs/interfaces.md` under `BlobStore`; neither can be checked from this crate.
- **Readiness of the mirrors is "holds a branch", not "is current".** `MirrorsReady` is the readiness check that fails `/readyz` until `Mirror::initialize` has filled an empty volume, so no read that needs a commit is routed to a replica that cannot answer. A mirror that has stopped fetching (a lost credential, an unreachable remote) stays ready; the signals are the webhook and poll outcomes and the fetch metrics, not readiness. A replica started on an empty volume while GitHub is down is not ready and takes no traffic until its first full fetch succeeds (bounded by `LK_GIT_FETCH_TIMEOUT_SECS`).
- **Buffers that hyper owns cannot be wiped (S7).** `Secret<T>` is redacted, zeroized on drop and compared in constant time, and the hub wipes the copies it controls (`GoogleResponse::wipe` overwrites an answer that carries a secret or a key when nothing else holds the bytes). The same bytes also pass through hyper, tonic, axum, rustls and prost buffers, reference-counted `Bytes` that are freed without zeroing: a join token inside a decoded gRPC frame, a bearer header, a Secret Manager answer or the webhook secret may stay in freed heap memory until it is reused, and a webhook body is read as plain bytes. Reading it needs access to the process's memory (a debugger, a core dump, the node); the pod has no shell and no capabilities (S16), and an administrator of the node is outside this model. This is the same limit the agent records for its key material.
- **Replica trust rests on KMS IAM on the replica CA key (A18).** Every replica signs its own certificate at start-up with the replica CA key, so "this is a hub replica" means "this process may use that key through Workload Identity". Anyone who can call `asymmetricSign` with that key can mint a replica certificate and forward commands to any agent stream, and the replica listener checks the SAN prefix but cannot tell a real replica from such a holder. The IAM binding on the replica CA key must go to the hub's service account alone and nothing else (09), and an audit log of KMS data access is the detection. The agent CA key has the same property for agents, with the 24 hour and ID-token limits above.
- **A stolen agent key is good for its 24 hours, and there is no revocation list.** A certificate is valid until it expires; the hub's controls are the short life, renewal only over the agent's own stream or a fresh join, the audit event for every issue, and disabling the swimlane's registered service account, which stops renewal. A stolen key can read and write that agent's NFS export within what the agent allows (typed paths, hashes, size caps) until then. Token-only clusters cannot rejoin without an Admin issuing a new token.
- **`POST /hooks/github` is the one unauthenticated HTTP route.** The controls are in the hub: the body cap, the HMAC over the raw bytes checked first and in constant time (a bare 401), the event type, the replay guard in Postgres, keyed on the HMAC of the body (the keys are pruned after seven days, so a capture replayed after that is accepted: for a push it costs one coalesced fetch, and for a `pull_request` it re-applies that event's action to its link, where `merged` stays terminal and nothing orders events by time; two deliveries whose bodies are byte for byte the same are one delivery to the guard, which GitHub is not expected to produce for distinct deliveries, as its bodies carry commit ids and timestamps; the cost of being wrong is one dropped duplicate, and the 60 s poll covers a push), a strict typed parse, and a bounded and coalesced fetch queue so a flood of valid deliveries cannot cause unbounded fetches. The source-address allowlist at the load balancer is the other control (S18, Q28), and it is 09's: the hub does no address check, so without it anyone who holds the secret, or who can flood the route with unsigned bodies up to the cap, reaches the HMAC step. A leaked webhook secret lets a holder trigger fetches and `GitHeadMoved` events for configured repositories only, never choose what is fetched. A push larger than the 1 MiB cap is a 413, and the 60 s poll picks the change up within 75 s.
- **`gix` brings `reqwest` and a wider dependency graph (A16).** The only `gix` HTTPS transport on rustls with aws-lc-rs is `reqwest`, so `reqwest`, `hyper-rustls` and `rustls-platform-verifier` are in `Cargo.lock` as `gix-transport`'s HTTP client; no crate of ours depends on `reqwest` or calls it. A human accepted this at the A16 gate (`plans/03b-hub-platform.blockers.md`) and chose to narrow the `[graph]` of `deny.toml` to the two Linux targets, so that `webpki-root-certs` (CDLA-Permissive-2.0, a wasm32-only dependency) is no longer checked. The cost is that `cargo deny` no longer checks the Windows and macOS dependency graphs, which no shipped binary uses; `cargo tree -i ring` for the hub-platform crate prints nothing. The normal graph of `hub-platform` grew from about 260 to about 410 unique crate versions, each pinned at least 14 days old and under `cargo audit`.
- **The hub binary has `ring` through `sqlx` for the database leg (A15).** The `sqlx` variants whose TLS is aws-lc-rs pull `webpki-roots` (CDLA-Permissive-2.0, which `deny.toml` does not allow), so the hub uses `tls-rustls-ring-native-roots`: the database connection is rustls on `ring`, while every listener and client the hub builds itself is rustls on aws-lc-rs. Two crypto providers are linked into one binary. The pool is always built with `verify-full` and the CA file from the settings (`sslrootcert`), whatever the URL says, so the platform roots that the feature name mentions are not what decides trust; this is proven against a Postgres with a wrong CA (`pg_pool_refuses_wrong_ca_and_wrong_host_name`) and would need a re-check on a `sqlx` upgrade.
- **`git_tree_index` has no retention.** The leaseholder writes the index of every branch head and tag commit once (one row per file of the commit's tree), and nothing deletes rows (D78 keeps versions but names no limit for this table), so the table grows with every commit pushed and every tag, by the number of files in the tree each time. The cache in memory is bounded by entries, but the table has no prune job and no partitioning, so disk and the cost of the indexes grow without a ceiling. A retention policy needs a decision (keep the heads, the baselines and the last N commits?) and is not made here; it is the first thing to settle before the repositories have a long history.
- **The Git client trusts the system roots and has a 30 s total timeout per request.** `gix`'s `reqwest` transport verifies GitHub's certificate against the platform's root store (it ignores `http.sslCAInfo`), so any root that the image's trust store holds can vouch for the remote, and the hub cannot pin GitHub's issuer; the distroless base carries Debian's CA bundle and receives fixes only when its digest is bumped. The transport's total timeout is 30 s for each request, a limit of the client that the hub cannot change, so a very large first fetch of a big repository over a slow link is made of requests that must each finish in 30 s; the hub's own deadline (120 s by default, plus a 45 s grace) bounds the whole fetch and a failure leaves the work to the next poll. The credential is only ever offered to the host the URL names.
- **Outbox rows and events are not content, but they are metadata.** An event names a swimlane, a path hash and a kind, and any active user at Viewer or above who may see the swimlane sees it; this is `EventVisibility`'s default (plan A10) and follows global roles. Replay reads the outbox for five minutes, and a replay follows the visibility rules at the time it is read; a live stream applies the rules of the user it was opened for until it ends (at most 30 minutes), so a change that only narrows what a user may see takes effect when the client reconnects.
- **Outbox delivery is at least once, and a slow commit is not delivered live.** A relay that has not yet read an older row hands it to a stream that subscribed in the meantime, even if that client already has it (an id below the cursor cannot be told from a late commit), so a client may see an event twice after resuming on another replica; the client's refetch is idempotent. A transaction that writes an event and takes longer than the relay's wait for a missing id (10 seconds) to commit is delivered to no live subscriber: its row stays, and only a replay (five minutes) or the next refetch finds it. The prune deletes rows after an hour, so a client that was away longer refetches. The relay's cursor on an outbox emptied by the prune restarts at 0, so the first event after a restart on an idle hub opens up to 4,096 expiring gaps for ten seconds, a bounded and harmless cost.
- **A batch is one statement, one notification per row, and rarely heard out of order.** `publish_many_in_tx` writes up to 200 events with one insert. The `outbox_notify` trigger is `FOR EACH ROW`, so the commit sends one notification per row, 200 at most per batch, where one per transaction would do; that takes a migration, and the relay only treats a notification as a prompt to look, so the cost is a few wake-ups that coalesce. A batch commits atomically and is read in id order, so it is heard in the order it was published in, with one exception that belongs to the relay and not to batching: while the relay works through a backlog of more than its read size (200 rows by default), ids that another transaction drew between the rows of the batch can turn the lower rows into gaps that are read in the next poll, after the higher ones. A single statement draws its ids one after the other, so that needs another session drawing ids at the same instant; events published one at a time in a transaction are far likelier to be apart. An event is a prompt to refetch, so the order within a batch does not decide what a client shows.
- **Delivery to a browser is at most once with a resync.** A subscriber that lags, a listener that reconnects and a replay window that is too old all end in a `Resync`, so a client must refetch; an event can be dropped between the commit and a subscriber's queue. Nothing that matters is kept only in an event, but a client that ignores `Resync` shows a stale screen.
- **An epoch race window of up to 30 seconds.** An agent that moves between replicas is found through `agent_connections`, cached for one second; a replica that dies without clearing its row is believed alive until its `last_seen_at` is 30 seconds old, and requests in that window fail as unavailable or are retried once on a fresh read. No request hangs, but a command may fail that a retry would have delivered.
- **Pull-request events can arrive out of order, and only the last one says where a link is.** GitHub does not order deliveries. A `closed` that overtakes the `reopened` before it leaves a link closed that is open on GitHub, until the next event for that pull request; `opened` is ignored for that reason (a link is created open, and acting on a late `opened` could undo a `closed`), and a merge is terminal whatever follows. The payload's own timestamps are not used. A link that is wrong is repaired by the next event for its pull request, and 06 can re-read a pull request from GitHub with the owning user's token.
- **A sentinel renews by joining again, and the audit action is the agent's.** The `Report` stream has no renewal message, so a sentinel asks for a new certificate with the VM's ID token every day, and each issue is one `agent_joined` audit event with a sentinel actor. `AuditAction` has no sentinel-specific variant (a new variant is a contract change that the plan did not ask for); the actor kind tells them apart in the log. The ID token's validity is the verifier's, as for agents.
- **A refused delta does not stop the stream.** The hub acknowledges what the sink applied and leaves the rest unacknowledged, so the agent resends it, but later messages on the same stream are still handed to the sink. Whether a delta whose predecessor was refused is applied is the sink's decision (05 compares the root a delta is based on with the one it holds and asks for a full scan on a mismatch); the hub does not hold back later messages, and an ack is for one `seq` and never means "everything before".
- **The ID token's validity is the verifier's.** The platform checks the email of the token against the registered service account, and the audience and signature through the `TokenVerifier` port (03a). A flaw in that port is a flaw in `Join`; the 24 hour certificate bounds what it buys.
- **The operations listener is plain HTTP with no authentication.** `/metrics` and `/readyz` are readable by whatever can reach the port; the NetworkPolicy contract limits ingress to the monitoring namespace, and a CNI that does not enforce NetworkPolicy removes that limit. The answers hold no secret, path or id.
- **The pod and network contracts depend on the cluster.** Whether the CNI enforces NetworkPolicy and FQDN policy, whether the image builds and runs as described, and whether base-image digests are refreshed are properties of the environment and of 09; `crates/hub/HARDENING.md` is the contract, and the tests lint the contract, not the cluster.

## Fact engine (04)

Owner: prompt 04. This section covers `crates/engine` as built by tasks T1 to T10: bytes, classes and line endings (T1); parsing, validation and flattening of YAML, JSON and `.properties` (T2); the settings index and locations, which hand the same text to tree-sitter (T3); paths, effective config and tree hashes (T4); drift, diffs and the grid (T5); checks C1 to C8 and effect previews (T6); the secret scan and text search (T7); the batch functions (T8); severity and inline ranges (T9); and the config-server's resolution model with C9 to C11 (T10). The engine is a library: it has no listener, no credential, no clock, no file or network access and no state. It runs inside the hub process, in the caller's `spawn_blocking`, on whatever the caller reads from the registry and the agents. So the questions are: can hostile *content* make it use unbounded time or memory, or crash the hub; can a result carry something that must not leave the hub; and can a wrong *fact* get past the people who rely on it. Who may ask is the caller's guard (S4); what is stored, audited or written is the caller's (05, 06, 07).

A row's Proof cell names tests, benchmarks or fuzz targets that exist in `crates/engine`; `tests/threat_model.rs` fails if one does not, or if a name is a helper and not a test.

### Assets

- **Config content.** File bytes and the settings parsed from them. The files are not meant to hold secrets (D18), but they can (keystores, a pasted token, a password in a value), so every value is treated as one (S13).
- **The hub's availability.** The engine runs on the hub's blocking threads; the nightly full recompute (P6) and a user's compare, grid or text search (P7) all use the same cores.
- **The correctness of facts.** Every view and every MCP answer is built from the engine's output (D33); a wrong drift state, effective hash, grid cell or finding is misleading in the way a wrong number is, and nobody sees it because the screen looks fine.
- **Redaction.** `secret_scan` decides what MCP output and edit warnings hide (S13, S14b). A match holds a line, a column, a length and a rule, never the value.
- **Determinism.** Identical input gives byte-identical output (acceptance 3), so a finding or a hash can be recomputed and compared.

### Trust boundaries

- **Callers to the engine.** The caller (05 for reads and recompute, 06 for edits, 07 for MCP) passes typed values (`NfsPath`, `LogicalFile`, `TenantId`, `TenantSet`, `ChannelName`, `AppName`, `ContentHash`, `Bytes`), never text that nobody has checked (S11). The engine does not authenticate or authorize: it trusts the caller to have done both, and returns facts for whatever it is given.
- **File content to the parsers.** Every byte of a config file is hostile input: it comes from NFS through an agent, from a Git mirror, from a past version, or from an upload that has not yet been confirmed. The Rust parsers read it first, under the limits; only text that they accepted goes on to tree-sitter, a C library (S22).
- **Patterns, globs and rule sets to the matchers.** A search pattern comes from a user or an MCP client; a deny glob, a host rule or a severity rule from an admin. The regex engine is linear-time with size limits (S11); globs are compiled with count and length limits.
- **Property sources to rendered output.** A resource is rendered with values from the property view, and the result is what a service would receive. The view's values are data: they are inserted once and never read as a template, and nothing is read from the process environment.
- **The engine to the log and to the result types.** Error values and `Debug` output carry sizes, names, paths and lines, never content (S21).

### STRIDE

| | Threat | Mitigation | Proof | S# |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S | A path is read as a file that it is not: a name that merely contains a tenant id is taken for that tenant's copy, a look-alike extension is taken for a property source, or `..` and `.git` components reach a mapping. | Paths are typed (`NfsPath`, `RepoPath`, `LogicalFile`) and refuse traversal and `.git` at construction. The reverse mapping strips only an exact `-<tenant>` of a known tenant right before the extension, never at the first hyphen. A file's role is a property source only for the exact stems and the three lower-case extensions of the config-server's rule (D82), and the engine's public functions take no raw string paths. | `hyphenated_names_not_split`, `orphan_suffix_is_untracked_base`, `hostile_paths_never_escape_mapping`, `forward_after_reverse_is_the_identity_prop`, `file_role_table`, `public_api_takes_no_raw_string_paths`, fuzz `engine_path_mapping` | S11 |
| T | A crafted file alters the result of a parser: a YAML alias or merge-key bomb, deep nesting, a huge scalar, a key that is longer than a setting path may be, a duplicate key that hides the real value, or a `${...}` run that makes rendering do much more work than the file is long. | Every size is bounded by `Limits` and checked at its boundary. Alias expansion is counted as nodes and never built past the budget. Duplicate keys and boolean-like values are parse *warnings* that the checks report (C8), and the last key wins as it does in the services. The placeholder scan looks a bounded distance ahead at each opener, rendering stops at the input limit, and a value that is inserted is never expanded again. The engine writes nothing, so there is no write to tamper with (no blind writes: 06 owns them). | `alias_bomb_returns_limit_error_fast`, `nodes_limit_is_enforced_at_boundary`, `scalar_limit_is_enforced_at_boundary`, `hostile_input_returns_error_not_panic`, `render_is_bounded`, `a_hostile_run_of_openers_is_linear_enough`, `value_containing_placeholder_not_re_expanded`, `duplicate_key_last_wins_with_warning`, fuzz `engine_yaml`, `engine_json`, `engine_properties`, `engine_render` | S11, S22 |
| R | Someone disputes a finding, a drift state or a hash, and it cannot be reproduced. | The engine is a pure function of its input: no clock (functions take `now` where time matters), no I/O, no random order (no `HashMap` or `HashSet`), no global state, and ordered parallel collection with explicit rayon pools. The same input gives the same bytes on 1, 2 and 8 threads, so any result can be recomputed from the stored inputs. The audit trail of a state change is the caller's (S9); the engine adds the reproducibility that the trail relies on. | `everything_twice_is_byte_identical`, `batch_matches_single_threaded_reference`, `no_hashmap_or_hashset_in_src`, `no_clock_or_io_in_src`, `search_truncation_is_deterministic` | S9, S21 |
| I | A value, a secret or a file's text leaves in an error, a log line, a `Debug` print, a finding or an MCP answer; a secret in a value is shown instead of hidden; a placeholder is filled from the process environment. | Error values are small `Copy` enums that name the limit and never the input. `Debug` of every type that holds content prints sizes. A check result holds paths, setting paths, lines, hashes and host names, never a value. `SecretMatch` stores no value, and `marker()` is the one place that formats `[REDACTED: N chars, rule]`. C10 takes environment variable *names* only and render takes no environment at all, so no environment value can be inserted or reported. Search hits do carry line text (it is the answer to a search); redacting them is the caller's step with `secret_scan` (S14b), and the scan's output replaces every span it finds. | `display_and_debug_never_echo_input`, `match_never_contains_value`, `marker_format`, `checks_c9_c10_c11_c10_results_never_hold_a_value`, `env_values_never_used`, `debug_never_prints_a_value`, `the_debug_output_of_a_blob_has_no_content`, `key_name_alone_not_flagged`, fuzz `engine_secret_scan` | S13, S14b, S21 |
| D | A pattern, a pair of files, a rule set or a swimlane is chosen to burn the hub's cores or memory: a catastrophic regex, a regex that compiles to a huge automaton, two 50,000-line files with a shared vocabulary and no shared skeleton, a glob list that explodes, a million placeholders openers, a swimlane with millions of files. | The regex engine is linear-time (`regex`, via ripgrep's matcher) with a pattern limit, a compiled-size limit, a DFA limit and a nesting limit. The diff has a line limit and a cell budget, and says "too large" the same way every time. Globs and rule sets have count and length limits. The batch refuses too many swimlanes or files before it starts. Every search and batch takes a cancel flag or a pool, and none of the limits is a wall-clock time, so the outcome does not depend on how busy the machine is. Benchmarks at target scale hold the budgets (P5 to P7). | `catastrophic_pattern_runs_linear`, `size_limit_and_dfa_limit_enforced`, `nest_limit_enforced`, `pattern_over_512_bytes_rejected`, `too_large_is_deterministic`, `cancel_flag_stops_work`, `hostile_lines_are_walked_in_linear_time`, `work_is_bounded_by_the_input_not_by_what_it_says`, `a_hostile_run_of_openers_is_linear_enough`, fuzz `engine_regex` | S11, S22 |
| E | A parser flaw turns a file into code execution or memory corruption in the hub process, or hostile content reaches a code path with more reach than it should have. | `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` in the crate, no `std::fs`, `std::net` or `std::process`, no tokio, no environment access, so the engine can only compute on what it is given. The one C component, tree-sitter, reads only bytes that the Rust parser accepted under the limits, runs with a progress callback that cancels it on a node budget, and is exercised under the fuzzer's sanitizers. Denied files (D79) are classified by name before their content is read, and neither search nor rendering looks inside one. | `forbid_unsafe_present`, `no_clock_or_io_in_src`, `the_alias_bomb_is_refused_before_anything_reads_it_twice`, `a_file_tree_sitter_cannot_read_is_indexed_from_the_parser_with_one_warning`, `denied_iff_the_matcher_says_so_prop`, `binary_not_rendered`, fuzz `engine_index_settings` | S21, S22 |

### Residual risks

- **tree-sitter is C code reading untrusted bytes inside the hub process.** The Rust parser and the limits stand in front of it, it is cancellable, and the fuzzer runs it under sanitizers, but a memory-safety flaw in the grammar's scanner or in the library would run with the hub's privileges. The mitigation is the pinned version (at least 14 days old, S19), `cargo audit` over both lockfiles, and keeping the C parser on input that is already known to be well-formed.
- **The resolution model is a reconstruction of the config-server's behaviour, and a wrong fact is the main risk (D86).** The property view, the choice of a resource and the substitution of placeholders follow D82 and D83; the points that the docs leave open are recorded as assumptions (plan A6, A10; the order of a channel request's candidates; the search order for resources, which the engine does not decide because a request names its logical file). Until the nightly "as served" differential check (D86, 05 and the agent) runs on real data, a difference would show up as a view that is wrong and looks right. The golden fixtures are reconstructions of the config-server's test data (plan A3), not copies.
- **The batch's effective hash of a property source has three layers.** A tenant file, the base file and the root `application.*` file feed the hash; `application-<tenant>.*` is part of `property_view` (and so of what C10 and render see) but not of the per-file hash in the batch. A change that only touches `application-<tenant>.*` is therefore seen by the property view and not by the tree hash of the file it overrides. 05 should either recompute through `property_view` for property sources or extend `Layers`; the engine does not hide this.
- **The secret scan is heuristic.** It looks for known prefixes, key headers, JWT shapes and long high-entropy strings, never at a key name, a placeholder or a public key. A secret in an unusual shape passes (a false negative), and a long random-looking public identifier blocks a save (Q20: six such files). The thresholds are configuration (`ScanConfig`) and are to be tuned with the Q20 files before 07 goes live.
- **Search hits carry content.** `search_text` returns line text because that is the answer; the engine does not redact it. Every caller that shows a hit to a person or an MCP client must pass it through `secret_scan` (S13, S14b). A caller that forgets is a leak, and the engine cannot see it.
- **Costs are bounded in size, not in time.** A hostile but valid input can still take as long as its limits allow (a 2 MiB file of openers costs about 256 steps per byte in the placeholder scan; a regex at the size limit scans linearly). The limits keep a single call to a bounded, predictable cost, but an attacker who can send many calls is stopped by the callers' rate limits and request limits (S4, 05), not by the engine.

## Registry and read API (05)

Owner: prompt 05. This section covers `crates/hub-registry` as built by tasks T1 to T10: ingestion of what the agents and the sentinel send, as source facts (T1, T7); the settings index and the Git side of drift (T2); the incremental pipeline that recomputes drift, findings and the effective tree with the engine (T3); the sync record and the sync windows that hold drift alerts (T4, T8); adoption, baselines and intentional divergence (T5); the read API, its caches and its text corpora (T6); attribution from the sentinel's records and from registered tool writes (T7, T1); severity, denied files, the blob policy and the retention job that deletes blobs and old rows (T9); and tenants, pickup states, the structure checks C9 to C11 and the nightly "as served" verification C12 (T10). It is a library: the hub's binary composes it (A8; the retention job is built and tested here and not yet registered there), and every route and port method it offers is behind a guard. It holds the facts that the whole product shows, so the questions are: can an agent or a hostile file make it store, show or conclude something false; can anything it holds leave where it must not (content, secrets, denied files, the names of environment variables' values); can a caller read or decide what its role does not allow; and can a burst, a large swimlane or a hostile query make it stop answering. Who is a person, and what the audit chain is, are 03a's; the mTLS identity of an agent and the blob endpoint are 03b's; what the engine computes is 04's; edits, uploads and the token vault are 06's.

A row's Proof cell names tests, benchmarks or fuzz targets that exist in `crates/hub-registry`; `tests/threat_model.rs` fails if one does not, or if a name is a helper and not a test.

### Assets

- **The facts about every swimlane's configuration.** The files of each swimlane as its agent reports them (`file_state`, `file_observations`, the blobs behind them), the Git side of drift, the baselines, the marks of an intentional divergence and the tenant sets. Files are not meant to hold secrets (D18), but they can (a keystore, a pasted token, a password in a value), so every value is treated as one (S13).
- **The derived facts.** The drift state of each file, the findings (C1 to C12), the effective tree hashes, the pickup state of each service. Everyone reads them as numbers, and a wrong one looks right (rule 7, D33): they are correct because they are recomputed by the engine from the source facts, and the registry must keep them so.
- **The decisions.** The adoption confirmation (baselines, tenant sets, editing unlocked), the marks, the severity rules, the sync windows. They are audited, and a wrong one changes what drift means.
- **Attribution.** Who made an out-of-band change: the OS Login user from the sentinel, a sync Job, the tool, or nobody known. It is evidence, and it is only as good as the weakest link that produced it.
- **Denied files' content (D79).** Keystores, private keys and any file the agent or the hub's globs deny. Only their name, size and hash are known to the hub, and no read path may give out more.
- **The blobs, and the rules that keep them.** Every version of every file that an agent has sent is a blob until the retention job deletes it, and a delete cannot be undone. What keeps a blob alive (a reference of any class, the keep rules of 50 versions and 180 days, the grace period after a store) is the asset: a hole in it loses the content that an edit, a baseline or a comparison needs.
- **The hub's availability and the P budgets.** Ingestion, the pipeline (P5, P6, P1, P2), the read API (P7) and the text corpora share the replica's cores, memory and the database.
- **What the config-server is seen to serve.** The agent's answer to `FetchServed` is evidence about a cluster the hub does not control; the C12 findings are derived from it.

### Trust boundaries

- **Agents and the sentinel to ingestion.** An agent is trusted to tell the truth about **its own swimlane** and about nothing else: its identity comes from the mutual TLS channel (03b, S5), the registry checks every message against it, and everything it reports (files, hashes, times, Deployments, sync windows) is data that is validated and bounded, not believed beyond its swimlane. A hostile or broken agent can still lie about its own swimlane's NFS; that is detected only by other sources (the sentinel, the Git side).
- **Users and MCP clients to the read API.** Every route and every port method is guarded by role (S4): 03a identifies the user, 03b's `GuardedRouter` applies the role, and the services check again before they look at an argument. Roles are global (D35): any active viewer reads every swimlane.
- **File content to the engine and the indexes.** Every byte of a config file is hostile input. The registry hands it to the engine under its limits, stores the settings that come out with flagged values redacted (D95), and never builds SQL, a path or a command from it.
- **Admin rows to the rules.** `severity_rules`, `path_mappings`, the deny globs, `folder_mappings` and the OS Login map are data: a row is parsed into a typed rule or skipped and counted, and an id or a description is never SQL (D103).
- **The agent's answer to `FetchServed` to the findings.** The answer is hashed in memory and dropped; it enters no log, metric, event or finding as text, and it is believed only for its own swimlane.
- **The registry to Postgres, the blob store and Git.** Postgres is trusted and reached as the `lanekeeper_app` role (least privilege: it cannot prune the sentinel's records except through the one function the job calls; it holds `DELETE` on `blobs`, `sync_windows` and `delta_staging` and the one column privilege `UPDATE (touched_at)` on `blobs`, which the blob store's `put` uses to refresh a blob's age); the blob store is content-addressed and read through the blob policy; the Git mirror is read through the `GitReader` port. Every call has a timeout, and every result set a bound.
- **Callers in the same process.** Prompt 06 registers its tool writes through `ports::ToolWrites` in its own transaction and is trusted to register only what it is about to write; the platform's blob endpoint asks the registry's policy before it serves a hash.

### STRIDE

| | Threat | Mitigation | Proof | S# |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S | An agent, or a process that has an agent's connection, sends facts as another swimlane, before it has said hello, or for a swimlane no admin created; a sentinel claims a swimlane or a root it was not registered for. | The identity comes from the channel (S5) and the registry compares every payload with it: a payload that names another swimlane is refused, nothing is accepted before `hello`, and `hello` is accepted only for a swimlane an admin created. A sentinel's `hello` must match the root the admin registered, its records are stored under the swimlane it serves and in no other, and a record outside the swimlane's prefix is dropped and counted. | `a_payload_that_names_another_swimlane_is_refused`, `a_swimlane_no_admin_created_is_refused`, `nothing_is_accepted_before_hello`, `one_swimlanes_deltas_do_not_touch_another`, `hello_is_accepted_only_for_a_sentinel_an_admin_registered_with_the_root_it_registered`, `a_record_attributes_in_the_swimlane_the_sentinel_serves_and_in_no_other`, `records_before_hello_or_from_a_sentinel_that_never_said_hello_are_refused` | S5, S25 |

| S | An agent answers a `FetchServed` with a lie, to raise a C12 that is not true or to hide one that is. | The request goes to the agent of the swimlane it is about, and the finding is written for that swimlane only: a lying agent changes the verdict of its own swimlane's combinations and no other's. The answer is hashed and dropped, so the lie can carry no text into a finding. The residual risk is stated below: the check is a smoke alarm and not a proof. | `a_lying_agent_can_raise_or_hide_a_finding_for_its_own_swimlane_and_for_no_other`, `served_text_is_data_whatever_it_says_it_only_changes_a_hash` | S5, S15 |
| T | A delta is replayed, duplicated, reordered, cut short or sent in parts that do not belong together (a spool replayed after an outage, a stream that ends early, parts of two deltas mixed), so that state moves back, a version is lost, or half a delta is applied. | A delta applies only with its last part and only if every part before it is there; a part sent again replaces itself; parts of different deltas are never mixed; an increment that does not start at the hub's root, or follows a lost stretch of the spool, asks for a full scan; a version older than the stored one is history and never moves the state back; the content is stored before the message is acknowledged, and a crash between chunks is repaired by the retry. | `a_second_delivery_of_the_same_delta_changes_nothing`, `a_version_older_than_the_stored_one_is_history_and_does_not_move_the_state_back`, `stream_end_before_the_last_part_applies_nothing`, `a_replayed_spool_loses_no_acknowledged_version`, `a_delta_that_does_not_start_at_the_hubs_root_asks_for_a_full_scan`, `a_lost_stretch_of_the_spool_asks_for_a_full_scan`, `a_crash_between_chunks_is_repaired_by_the_retry`, `parts_of_different_deltas_are_not_mixed`, `content_is_stored_before_the_message_is_acknowledged`, fuzz `registry_delta` | S5, S11, S22 |
| T | A hostile agent sends paths that escape a tree, content that does not match its hash or size, an entry count or size that exhausts memory, or a time in the future; a sentinel sends a record whose path is a traversal. | Every entry is validated before anything is stored: a hash or size that does not match the bytes is invalid, a path the mapping cannot place is not a file, sizes and counts are bounded, a time ahead of the hub's clock is clamped to it, and an invalid message stores nothing. The sentinel's paths are mapped by component, never as text, and a root that is not a clean absolute path maps nothing. | `bytes_that_do_not_match_their_hash_or_size_are_invalid`, `size_and_count_limits`, `times_are_range_checked_and_the_future_is_clamped`, `invalid_messages_store_nothing`, `size_and_entry_limits_are_enforced`, `a_time_in_the_future_is_clamped_to_the_hubs_clock`, `hostile_paths_are_dropped_or_cleaned_and_never_survive_with_a_traversal`, `the_prefix_is_compared_by_component_and_not_as_text`, `an_export_with_a_traversal_does_not_become_a_prefix_that_matches_other_trees`, fuzz `registry_sentinel_path` | S11, S22 |
| T | The derived facts drift from the truth: an event is missed, a replica takes over, a rule changes, or the incremental code has a flaw, so that a wrong drift state, finding or tree hash is shown and nobody sees it. | Facts come only from the engine (rule 7, D33). The incremental state is held equal to a full recompute by a property test over random event sequences and after a takeover; a reconcile finds what no event told the pipeline; a change of the rules recomputes the whole swimlane; and the nightly job recomputes everything, repairs any row that differs and counts it. | `incremental_state_equals_full_recompute_after_random_events`, `incremental_equals_full_after_a_takeover`, `the_reconcile_finds_what_no_event_told_the_pipeline`, `a_change_of_rules_recomputes_the_whole_swimlane_and_not_the_paths`, `nightly_full_recompute_repairs_a_corrupted_row`, `sync_repairs_a_table_that_was_changed_behind_its_back`, `incremental_writes_equal_a_full_write_after_random_edits` | S21 |
| T | A sync Job or a person overwrites an NFS change nobody reviewed (C7), or drift alerts are held by sync windows so that out-of-band changes are not announced. | The sync record compares each file with the Git side its name stands for and raises C7 for a change made over an NFS-only one; a window holds a swimlane's alerts only until it is processed, never longer than `sync_window_stale_after` or `sync_close_give_up`, and all of a swimlane's windows together hold them for at most `sync_hold_budget` in any rolling day (D102), with a notice once the budget is spent. | T | Two confirmations of one adoption pass, or a file that changes while the confirmation waits, commit a baseline that nobody reviewed; a failure half way leaves some baselines set. | The confirmation takes the swimlane's lock and checks every resolution against the versions it was made for: of two confirmations exactly one commits, a file that changed since it was reviewed needs a new decision and aborts the confirmation whole, and a failure anywhere leaves nothing behind (one transaction with its audit event). | `of_two_confirmations_of_one_pass_exactly_one_commits`, `a_decision_changed_while_the_confirmation_waits_is_not_applied_unseen`, `a_file_that_changes_while_the_confirmation_waits_for_the_lock_aborts_it_whole`, `a_failure_anywhere_in_the_confirmation_leaves_nothing_behind`, `a_file_that_changed_since_it_was_reviewed_needs_a_new_decision` | S9 |
| T | An admin's row, a search needle or a path is built into SQL, or a severity row is read as code; a `LIKE` pattern is smuggled through a name. | Queries are runtime-checked with binds; SQL built with `format!` takes only the module's own time helpers (a source scan fails on anything else, and on `OFFSET`, an unbounded channel or a direct publish). | T | Someone registers a tool write to make an out-of-band change look like the tool's, or a registration explains more than the one write it was made for. | Registrations come from the process that owns the write path, in its transaction; a registration explains at most one observation, of exactly its swimlane, path and hash, within its time to live by the registry's clock, never one applied before it was stamped, and never replaces an equal or stronger attribution. A registration that was rolled back explains nothing, and a registered hash is not a version of its path and its blob is not served. | `the_same_hash_at_another_path_or_in_another_swimlane_does_not_match`, `a_registration_that_was_rolled_back_explains_nothing`, `an_expired_registration_takes_nothing`, `an_observation_applied_before_the_registration_was_stamped_is_not_the_tools_write`, `a_registration_attributes_at_most_one_observation`, `a_registered_hash_is_not_a_version_of_its_path_and_its_blob_is_not_served`, `the_matcher_upgrades_what_is_weaker_and_never_replaces_a_tool_write` | S9 |
| T | Blob garbage collection deletes a blob that something still needs: one that a reference class names (a baseline, a current file row, a proposal, a draft, a PR link, a tool-write registration, a staged delta, a `blob_refs` row), a version that the keep rules keep, or one whose hash sits in a column that a later migration added and nobody told the job about. | The job deletes by SQL under the app role's own `DELETE` grant (D116), and a blob goes only when three tests hold together, repeated in the statement that deletes it: its grace has passed, no reference class names it (one probe for each column that can name a blob), and the keep rules do not keep it (one of the last 50 versions of its file or newer than 180 days, read from `retention_settings`; a blob that any version of any file has is kept). The foreign key of `blob_refs` is the backstop for a reference that arrives while the blob goes. Every column of the schema that can hold a hash is decided in one table (`DECISIONS`), a test holds the table to the live catalog both ways, and a column added later fails the gate until someone decides it. An audit event's hashes do not pin a blob; only an explicit `blob_refs` row does (D116). | `gc_never_deletes_a_referenced_blob`, `every_reference_class_pins_and_every_look_alike_does_not`, `every_column_that_can_hold_a_hash_is_decided`, `a_column_added_later_fails_the_check_until_it_is_decided`, `the_columns_that_are_read_are_the_columns_the_sql_reads`, `every_column_that_is_read_has_a_class_that_proves_it_and_every_look_alike_is_decided_as_not_reading`, `every_decision_that_is_not_a_reference_says_why`, `keeps_the_last_50_versions_and_180_days`, `the_keep_rules_are_the_settings_row_and_q33_when_there_is_none`, `a_blob_is_not_collected_before_its_grace_period_has_passed`, `a_longer_grace_period_is_the_configurations`, `an_audit_events_hashes_do_not_pin_a_blob_only_a_blob_refs_row_does`, `a_denied_files_old_blob_is_collected_like_any_other_once_beyond_the_keep_rules` | S21, S11 |
| T | A writer has stored a blob and not yet committed the row that references it (ingestion, a tool write, an adoption, a PR job), or a put, a referencing writer or a new reference arrives between the job's plan and its delete, so that a blob that was collectable a moment ago is deleted. | The plan is a hint made without locks and the delete tests everything again. The age is tested in the same statement that locks and deletes the row, never from an earlier read, and a put of an old blob refreshes it (`touched_at`, migration 0008), so a stored blob is protected for the grace period (48 hours, never less than the touch interval plus the staging lifetime: the configuration is refused below it). The job locks first (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`) and deletes in the same transaction as a statement of its own, so that its snapshot sees every reference committed so far: a blob that a put or a referencing writer holds is skipped and left for the next run, a put that finds the delete in progress waits and stores the blob again, the foreign key refuses a reference that arrives during the delete, and `lock_timeout` is set for the transaction and never for the session. | `a_put_between_the_plan_and_the_delete_protects_the_blob`, `an_ingest_that_stored_an_old_blob_and_has_not_yet_committed_its_reference_keeps_it`, `a_blob_that_a_put_is_refreshing_is_skipped_and_nobody_waits`, `a_put_that_finds_the_delete_locked_waits_and_the_blob_is_stored_again`, `a_reference_that_appears_between_the_plan_and_the_delete_keeps_the_blob`, `a_new_observation_of_the_same_content_after_the_plan_keeps_the_blob`, `a_writer_that_is_referencing_a_blob_makes_the_collector_skip_it`, `the_foreign_key_refuses_a_reference_that_arrives_while_the_blob_is_being_deleted`, `lock_timeout_leaves_the_sessions_setting_as_it_found_it_and_a_blocked_chunk_waits_for_the_next_run`, `the_grace_period_is_range_checked_against_the_contract_of_put` | S21, S11 |
| T | The prune of `sync_windows` or the staging sweep removes what is still read: a window the hold budget still counts, a window nobody has processed, or a part of a delta that is still arriving. | Only a processed window whose every time is older than `sync_window_retention` goes (7 days; the configuration is refused unless that is longer than two rolling hold windows plus `sync_window_stale_after` and `sync_close_give_up`), in statements of at most `retention_prune_batch` rows and at most `retention_prune_batches` of them a run, and a window that is not processed is never pruned. The hold budget gives the same answer before and after a prune (a property test, and to the millisecond for a spent budget). The staging sweep deletes only parts older than `staging_ttl`, and the job refuses an ingestion with another staging lifetime, so that the grace period and the sweep agree. | `only_processed_windows_older_than_the_retention_in_every_time_are_pruned`, `a_run_prunes_at_most_batch_times_batches_windows_and_the_rest_waits`, `the_hold_budget_answers_the_same_before_and_after_a_prune`, `a_spent_budget_is_spent_the_same_way_after_the_prune`, `a_retention_of_windows_that_the_budget_still_reads_is_refused_at_construction`, `the_retention_of_sync_windows_is_longer_than_the_hold_budget_reads_back`, `stale_staged_parts_are_swept_in_batches_and_the_ones_within_the_ttl_stay`, `the_sweep_on_hello_is_bounded_the_same_way_and_leaves_the_rest`, `a_stale_part_stops_pinning_its_blob_in_the_run_that_sweeps_it_and_a_fresh_one_does_not`, `a_job_over_an_ingestion_with_another_staging_ttl_is_refused` | S11, S21 |
| R | A state change has no audit event, or an event has no change (a crash between the two), or a change is announced that never committed. | Every audited action (D93: an out-of-band change, a baseline adoption, a mark and its clear, an attribution upgrade, a tenant-set change, a deployment record) writes its audit event in the transaction of the change; every event goes through `publish_in_tx` into the outbox, never directly, so a rolled-back transaction announces nothing and a retry announces once. | `an_out_of_band_change_is_audited_and_its_author_is_unknown`, `a_denied_file_changing_is_audited`, `an_upgrade_writes_one_audit_event_and_one_attribution_updated`, `a_scan_that_cannot_be_audited_announces_nothing_and_the_retry_announces_it_once`, `every_event_goes_through_publish_in_tx`, `a_rolled_back_transaction_leaves_no_event_and_a_committed_one_leaves_each`, `a_failing_bus_rolls_the_whole_report_back` | S9 |
| R | A change is attributed to the wrong person, or an attribution is lowered or lost, so that "who did this" cannot be answered. | Attribution only goes up: a strictly higher confidence replaces, an equal one never does, and a tool write or a sync Job's change is never touched by a sentinel record. A sentinel record explains a change within sixty seconds, on the same path, if it succeeded and names someone, and the order in which the scan, the record and the job arrive does not change the answer; an upgrade and its events are one transaction. | `an_upgrade_is_never_a_downgrade`, `a_local_vm_edit_is_sentinel_login_high_and_names_the_user`, `a_sync_window_change_is_sync_job`, `an_nfs_write_outside_any_job_is_nfs_client`, `the_order_in_which_the_scan_the_record_and_the_job_come_does_not_change_the_answer`, `a_record_explains_a_change_within_sixty_seconds_inclusive_and_not_beyond`, `an_upgrade_and_its_events_are_one_transaction_and_the_batch_is_acknowledged_only_after_it_commits` | S9, S25 |
| R | A finding is disputed and cannot be reproduced, or disappears with no trace of why. | Findings are keyed by a fingerprint of their kind and what they are about: one that stays keeps its row and its `detected_at`, one that goes is resolved and never deleted, and a computation of a swimlane that is already right writes nothing. The same files give the same findings cold and warm (the structure checks' cache is held to a cold computation after every kind of change). | `a_finding_keeps_its_row_while_the_same_problem_lasts_and_is_resolved_when_it_goes`, `a_second_whole_computation_of_a_swimlane_that_is_right_writes_nothing`, `the_finding_clears_when_the_config_server_matches_again_and_a_changed_answer_updates_it`, `running_it_twice_changes_nothing_the_second_time`, `a_cold_computation_says_what_the_warm_one_did_after_every_kind_of_change`, `every_structure_kind_has_an_identity_of_its_own_that_does_not_move_with_the_others` | S9, S21 |
| R | Blobs are deleted and nobody can say that the job did it, or how much. | A run reports what it did in counts and bytes, in the log and in the `registry_retention_*` metrics (blobs deleted, skipped and contended, rows pruned, runs, dry runs, truncated runs, failed steps), and a dry run reports what it would have done and changes nothing. The chain is never touched. A collection is not an audit event (A12, human-approved, D118): the residual risks below say what that leaves open. | `dry_run_deletes_nothing`, `a_dry_run_counts_the_windows_and_leaves_them`, `a_dry_run_counts_the_stale_parts_and_leaves_them`, `no_hash_path_or_content_reaches_a_log_line_a_metric_or_an_error`, `an_audit_events_hashes_do_not_pin_a_blob_only_a_blob_refs_row_does` | S9, S10 |
| I | File content, a setting value, a secret, an environment value or a served text reaches a log line, an error, a metric, an event, a finding or a notification. | Logs carry ids, paths, hashes and counts; errors are closed enums that carry no input and are mapped at the edge to a problem with a code; a finding holds names, counts and ids (C10 holds the names of keys and the lines that name them, never a value; C12 holds two hashes); metrics have closed labels; notifications name a swimlane, a count and a time. | I | A denied file's content leaves through a read path, a comparison, a search, a rendering, the blob endpoint, the agent, a log or a finding. | One rule (`is_denied`: the flag, the class, or the hub's globs) is applied wherever one file is decided: no route returns content, a denied file is compared by hash and size alone, its Git side is never read, a search never looks inside it, a denied property source gives no value to the text it would have filled in, the blob policy applies the same rule to every file that has had the hash, now or ever (the flag, the class, and the hub's own globs, also when the agents have not reported the files since the globs changed and also for a file that was deleted), refuses a hash that too many files have had to decide, fails closed, and answers as for an unknown hash, the agent is never asked to serve it, and a change of the flag is itself a change of the file. | `a_denied_files_bytes_leave_through_no_read_path_and_reach_no_log_error_or_metric`, `a_denied_file_never_returns_content_through_any_route`, `the_blob_policy_refuses_their_hashes`, `a_hub_whose_globs_changed_before_the_agent_said_so_refuses_the_blob_by_hash`, `a_denied_files_versions_are_refused_after_the_file_is_deleted_and_the_pipeline_dropped_its_row`, `content_withheld_has_no_bytes`, `a_file_that_becomes_denied_is_denied_from_then_on_and_one_that_stops_being_denied_is_readable_again`, `a_denied_property_source_gives_no_value_to_the_text_it_would_have_filled_in`, `a_denied_file_is_compared_by_hash_and_its_git_content_is_never_read`, `served_withholds_a_denied_file_and_never_asks_the_agent_for_it`, `a_denied_file_is_never_asked_for_and_never_named`, `the_platforms_blob_endpoint_serves_an_ordinary_blob_and_answers_a_denied_hash_as_it_answers_an_unknown_one` | S10, S17 |
| I | The swimlane list tells every viewer who confirmed a baseline, and the tree, the searches and the findings name a file's logical file and tenant, in a way that gives away more than the response shows already (`contract/08-web` CG2, CG4a, Q6, D125, D126). | The person is a `UserRef` of the user's id and display name, never the email, the GitHub login or the role (Q6, which the human approved for every signed-in user); the logical file and the tenant are derived from the file's name and the registry's classification of it, which every listing shows already, so a denied file's say nothing of its content and no search lists a denied file; the project, the tier, the pickup counts and the sync Job's name are configuration and cluster facts that the swimlane list shows already in other forms (the events carry the Job). | `a_baseline_is_unconfirmed_until_editing_is_unlocked_with_a_time_and_names_who_confirmed_it`, `a_denied_file_names_its_logical_file_and_tenant_from_its_name_and_nothing_of_its_content`, `the_tree_names_the_logical_file_and_the_tenant_of_every_file_and_none_for_a_folder` | S4, S17 |
| I | A caller who knows a hash reads a blob it should not: a draft, a registered but unwritten version, or an old version of a file under another path. | A blob is served only under a path that had it; a hash that was only registered for a tool write is not a version of its path; the blob policy answers a denied hash as an unknown one. A file that is flagged denied and has the content now settles it in one probe of a partial index (`file_state_denied_hash_idx`), however many files have had that content. Otherwise it reads the files that have had the content (their paths, flags and classes, by the observations' hash index, at most `HASH_PATHS_LIMIT` of them) and applies the one rule to each, so the hub's own globs, the earlier versions of a file that is denied now, and a file whose row is gone are decided as every other read decides them; a hash that more files have had than that is refused as undecided (counted, with at most a debug line), and a question that cannot be asked or read is refused too. | I | The agent's served text, or the engine's rendering, is kept in a finding to show "what differs", and it holds a value. | A C12 does not say where two texts differ and does not keep either of them: it holds the application, the tenant, the channel, the file, the reason and the two hashes.
