| D | A burst of changes, a large swimlane or a rule change makes the pipeline fall behind: every event recomputes everything, or the structure checks slow each batch. | Events for one swimlane are coalesced in short windows, only the paths an event names are recomputed, and a batch sends a fixed number of statements however large the swimlane is; C9 to C11 run in a leased sweeper and never inside a batch, are cached by content, and read only what moved. The budgets are benchmarks. | `a_burst_of_changes_to_one_swimlane_is_one_batch`, `a_head_that_moves_recomputes_the_paths_of_its_diff_and_nothing_else`, `a_change_recomputes_only_the_path_that_changed`, `the_statements_of_a_one_file_change_do_not_depend_on_the_size_of_the_swimlane`, `a_one_file_change_sends_the_statements_it_always_sent_and_no_structure_check`, `a_swimlane_nothing_moved_in_is_not_read_and_a_second_pass_changes_nothing`, `a_one_file_change_reads_one_blob_and_evaluates_one_file`, `incremental_recompute_base`, `incremental_recompute_per_file_per_swimlane`, `full_recompute_target_scale`, `delta_to_drift_changed`, `head_moved_to_drift` | S11 |
| D | A query, a pattern, a page size or a search burns the hub: a catastrophic regex, an unbounded list, a scan of a large table, a search that outlives its caller. | Every query is bounded or keyed and the plans of the hot ones are snapshotted (no sequential scan of a large table); pagination is mandatory and capped; patterns are bounded and run in linear time under size limits; a text search has a deadline and a ceiling on hits, answers 503 past it and does not hold the pool; the corpus is built once for a version and one that is over its bound is made for the request and not kept. | `read_queries_have_no_sequential_scan_on_large_tables`, `structure_queries_are_bounded_and_read_no_large_table_by_a_scan`, `a_page_is_a_limit_of_one_to_five_hundred_and_a_well_formed_cursor`, `the_whole_query_is_bounded_and_must_decode`, `a_pattern_is_bounded_and_a_regex_runs_in_linear_time_under_limits`, `a_search_that_runs_past_its_deadline_is_answered_503_and_the_pool_is_not_held`, `the_caps_are_the_servers_ceilings_whatever_the_request_asks_and_truncation_is_reported`, `a_snapshot_over_its_bound_is_made_for_the_request_and_not_kept`, `concurrent_cold_searches_share_one_corpus_build`, fuzz `registry_query` | S11, S22 |
| D | The served route or the nightly verification floods an agent and the config-server behind it, or one starves the other. | One round trip takes at most `served_timeout`; at most `served_concurrency` are in flight on a replica and the rest are refused, not queued; the verification asks one request at a time for a swimlane, a pause apart, at most four swimlanes at once, waits for a slot for a bounded time and gives the combination up after that, and counts a timeout instead of retrying. | `a_silent_agent_is_given_the_timeout_and_no_more_than_the_limit_are_in_flight`, `served_is_limited_to_the_concurrency_and_the_timeout_of_the_rest_route`, `one_request_at_a_time_to_a_swimlane_and_the_swimlanes_are_asked_side_by_side`, `a_request_waits_for_a_slot_of_the_replica_and_gives_the_combination_up_when_none_comes_free`, `samples_200_combinations_per_swimlane`, `a_timeout_is_counted_and_what_stood_stays` | S11, S17 |
| D | Hostile config text makes the structure checks, the settings index or the pickup computation expensive, or a job runs for ever or after it lost its lease. | Every check is bounded by the engine's limits and by the registry's (files, folders, services, findings, channels); results are cached by content so a change costs what it touches; every job asks its lease before each unit of work and stops when it is lost, and a run is bounded in swimlanes and in time. | `the_sweeper_stops_when_the_lease_is_lost`, `the_nightly_job_runs_the_recompute_and_stops_when_its_lease_is_gone`, `the_matcher_stops_when_the_lease_is_lost_before_it_writes`, `a_lost_lease_ends_the_run_and_the_next_one_starts_from_the_database`, `the_job_has_a_spec_a_name_and_a_lease_that_ends_the_run`, `the_sweeper_is_bounded_and_goes_on_where_it_stopped`, `settings_index_row_count_matches_the_engine` | S11, S21 |
| D | A retention run does too much or holds too much: a plan of the whole store, a delete that waits behind a writer, two replicas running the job, a run that goes on after it lost its lease, or a dry run that is not dry. | A run plans at most `gc_batch` blobs (500) and `gc_max_bytes` (64 MiB) in hash order, deletes them in transactions of `gc_chunk` (100) and leaves the rest for the next run. The plan is a read-only transaction under `gc_scan_timeout` and takes no lock; a chunk that waits past `gc_lock_timeout` is left and counted, not retried for ever. The job is leased (one holder), checks the lease before every statement that writes and ends the run when it is lost. A dry run does the same looking and changes nothing, takes no lock and leaves no new row version, and a second run finds nothing new to do. The configuration is refused outside its bounds. | `a_run_takes_at_most_gc_batch_blobs_and_the_rest_waits_for_the_next`, `a_run_takes_at_most_gc_max_bytes`, `a_run_that_deleted_everything_has_nothing_to_do_the_second_time`, `dry_run_deletes_nothing`, `a_dry_run_takes_no_lock`, `the_job_runs_through_the_runners_context`, `a_run_stops_at_the_next_chunk_when_the_lease_is_lost`, `only_the_lease_holder_runs_the_job`, `the_job_is_named_after_its_lease_and_the_default_config_makes_a_valid_spec`, `every_retention_bound_is_enforced` | S11, S21 |
| D | A real collection removes a blob that a read, a search or a shard being built is fetching, so that the read fails or the answer is wrong. | A read of a version whose blob is gone is a clean `NotFound` for that version and never a failure of anything else; a search that is fetching a blob when it goes leaves that file out, with its places, and answers with the rest; after a collection every other read gives what it gave before. A blob that lingers in one replica's `PgBlobStore` cache is immutable, so what it serves is still the bytes of its hash. | `a_search_that_is_fetching_a_blob_that_a_real_collection_deletes_still_answers`, `after_a_real_collection_everything_reads_as_before_and_the_collected_version_is_not_found` | S21, S11 |
| E | A route, a port method or a service call has no guard, or asks for less than the role it should: a viewer starts an adoption, marks a divergence or reads what only an editor may. | The route table is generated from the contract and tested: every route is behind a guard and no route asks for more than the contract says; adoption needs an active admin and the marks an operator, checked on the service before any database call; the port's methods refuse a user who may not read before they look at an argument. | `registry_route_table_is_guarded_and_matches_openapi`, `a_route_cannot_be_mounted_without_a_guard_by_construction`, `the_harness_rejects_an_unguarded_or_wrongly_guarded_registry_route`, `every_role_that_can_sign_in_reads_every_route_and_no_route_asks_for_more`, `the_routes_are_exactly_the_fifteen_read_operations_of_the_contract`, `only_an_active_admin_can_run_or_confirm`, `the_marks_need_an_operator_and_say_so_before_they_look`, `the_check_is_on_the_service_and_comes_before_any_database_call`, `every_role_that_may_read_may_ask_the_six` | S4 |
| E | Config text, a served answer, an admin's rule or a finding's message is read as an instruction by a person, a tool or an LLM downstream. | Config and served text is data: it is hashed, parsed into settings or shown as text with its role and never interpreted; a finding's message is built from names the hub checked (application, tenant, channel and file names are validated tokens and paths) and no content; the MCP layer labels what it sends as data (07). | `served_text_is_data_whatever_it_says_it_only_changes_a_hash`, `what_the_agent_and_the_config_server_say_become_our_statuses_and_their_words_go_nowhere`, `what_a_rule_says_is_data_an_id_is_never_sql_and_a_description_is_never_read`, `a_notification_names_ids_and_counts_and_no_file` | S15, S10 |
| E | A flaw in a parser or a query turns hostile content into code execution or memory corruption in the hub process, or the application role is used to change what it should only read. | `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`, no panicking constructs outside tests (scans that fail on a planted violation), fuzz targets for the registry's own parsers (cursors, deltas, queries and sentinel paths) with a seed replay in the gate, and a database role that cannot prune the sentinel's records except through the one function the job calls. | `forbid_unsafe_present`, `no_panic_outside_tests`, `panic_scan_finds_planted_violations`, `corpus_replay_registry_cursor`, `corpus_replay_registry_delta`, `corpus_replay_registry_query`, `corpus_replay_registry_sentinel_path`, `the_function_the_job_calls_is_the_only_way_the_app_prunes_records`, `arbitrary_bytes_as_a_cursor_are_total` | S21, S22, S25 |
| E | The verification, or any registry job, writes or sends more than it should: a write command to an agent, a direct write to a table it does not own, a second writer of findings. | The only command the verification sends is `FetchServed`, built in one place; its findings go through the one findings writer (one transaction under the swimlane's findings lock, an event in the outbox); a source scan fails on a write verb, a direct publish or another command in the module. | `the_verification_reads_the_config_server_and_writes_findings_only_through_the_findings_store`, `samples_200_combinations_per_swimlane`, `nothing_but_the_event_bus_is_needed_to_publish`, `no_source_writes_the_git_tree_index` | S9, S21 |
| E | The retention job is used to change what the app role should only read, or needs more than the app role has: a superuser, a truncate, an alter, a write to a table it is not named for, a session setting left on the pooled connection. | The job runs as `lanekeeper_app` and needs only the grants that role has (`DELETE` on `blobs`, `sync_windows` and `delta_staging`); it sets no session setting (`lock_timeout` and `statement_timeout` are set for the transaction only), and a test compares every table of the database, with its row versions, before and after a run to show that a run changes no table but the ones it is named for. | `the_job_runs_as_the_app_role_and_needs_nothing_the_app_role_does_not_have`, `a_run_changes_no_table_but_the_ones_it_is_named_for`, `lock_timeout_leaves_the_sessions_setting_as_it_found_it_and_a_blocked_chunk_waits_for_the_next_run` | S21, S4 |

### Residual risks

- **Parallel swimlane commits are not atomic together.** The pipeline commits the swimlanes of one batch in parallel (`persist_concurrency`, 4 by default), each in a transaction of its own with its events. Each swimlane is consistent with itself; a crash or a failure between two commits leaves some swimlanes at the new version and others at the old one, and the next batch, the reconcile or the nightly run repairs it. There is no atomicity across swimlanes, so a view that spans swimlanes (the grid, a cross-swimlane compare) can for a moment mix versions.
- **Adoption can pick a wrong base commit, and its secret scan can say `unscanned`.** The base-commit search is bounded by its candidate count and by the paths whose history it reads: a commit that is exact but untagged and not the head can be missed, and the search then settles for the best match among the commits it tried, so the review shows more differing files than there are (a human decides each). A file over the scan's size limit, or one that cannot be decoded, is listed as `unscanned` and not as clean, and Git files over the limit are not read; the adoption pass therefore does not prove that both repositories are free of secrets.
- **Raw file text, blobs, diff hunks and search lines are not masked here (A28).** This crate masks flagged values in the surfaces whose subject is a setting value and nowhere else: the text of a file, a blob, the hunks of a comparison, the lines of a text search and the text of a served file (the config-server's copy) carry what is in the file (D18: config has no secrets; an edit needs the bytes). This is a gap against S14b ("the web UI masks flagged values by default") that prompts 06, 07 and 08 must close or accept, and the engine's text search says in its own residual risks that its callers must pass hits through the scan. `contract/08-web` (CG7, option M1) now assigns the masking at the REST edge, its scan cache and benchmark, the `masks` lists and the blob policy's refusal of a flagged text file to this crate: none of it is built yet (D128), so the gap stands until it is.
- **`only_differing` hides some changes of a secret.** The grid's `only_differing` compares what a cell shows, and a flagged value is shown as `[REDACTED: N chars, rule]`: a change from one secret to a secret of the same length and rule looks like no change and the row is hidden. The values are compared by hash everywhere else (a changed secret does not look unchanged in a comparison), so only this filter has the blind spot.
- **A one or two character needle is a repeatable scan.** A settings or text search with a very short needle matches almost everything and costs 220 to 290 ms of the replica's read pool on every request (measured at the target scale); it is bounded by the hits ceiling, the deadline and the pool, but any viewer can repeat it, and the registry has no rate limit of its own (S4's guard says who, the MCP layer's per-user limits and the platform's say how often).
- **The text corpora are memory: 67.9 MiB per replica at the target scale, capped at 256 MiB.** With three replicas that is up to three copies of the same snapshot, each within its own cap; a snapshot over the cap is made for the request and not kept, so the cost then moves from memory to latency.
- **Staleness is bounded by an assumption.** A search never reads data older than its swimlane's version, and that holds if the swimlane's version moves with or after its files (the pipeline stamps it in the transaction that writes them; ingestion's own writes are followed by the pipeline's). A writer that changed files without moving the version would leave a search on an older list until the next batch.
- **Cursors are bound to their query, not signed.** A cursor is a canonical encoding of a key and the hash of the query it belongs to, and it has no signature: a caller can craft one for the same query, and gets only a different page of what the query would return to them anyway. Tampering with the context is refused; there is no secret to leak, and the property is that a cursor cannot reach another query's or swimlane's data.
- **The served route's slots are shared and a viewer can fill them.** At most 16 `FetchServed` round trips are in flight on a replica, shared by the REST route, the MCP `served` tool and the nightly verification; a viewer (or a script) that asks for 16 served files at once holds all the slots for up to the 10 s timeout, the next request is refused (503), and the verification waits for a slot for a bounded time and then leaves that combination for the next night. The agent adds its own limit of five a second.
- **There are two drift lists in two orders.** The port's `drift` lists files by path and REST's lists them by severity, most serious first (D108); the two have their own cursors, a cursor of one is refused by the other, and a client that wants one order must use that route. No data differs between them.
- **Alert-hold chaining is capped, but a compromised agent controls the window events.** A sync window holds a swimlane's drift alerts, and the windows are whatever the agent reports in its cluster report. D102 caps the time they can hold in total (`sync_hold_budget`, 4 h in any rolling 24 h per swimlane) and says so once, but within that budget a compromised agent can make out-of-band changes and have their alerts held, and the closing evaluation then alerts what it held (a duplicate at worst, never a loss, D99). The cap is per swimlane and a person has to read the notice.
- **A33: baselines can be recorded at the wrong commit if Git moves between the sync and the close.** When a window closes, `record_sync` is called with the current heads of the mirrored base branch and of the tenant branches (no explicit commits exist until prompt 10 chooses a reporting option), so a head that moved between the sync and the close records a baseline at a commit that the sync did not apply; the files then look in sync with a version they were not synced from. A later drift evaluation is against the baseline.
- **The `sync_windows` prune can leave a late observation unattributed, and a window that was never closed is never pruned (D117).** The retention job deletes the processed windows whose every time is older than `sync_window_retention` (7 days by default), and the hold budget answers the same before and after (a property test). Ingestion attributes an observation to a sync Job by the window that contains the time the agent saw it, so an observation that is reported more than `sync_window_retention` after it was seen (an agent that was offline for over a week and then replays its spool) finds no window and stays out of band, unattributed to its Job; the change is still recorded and audited. A window that was never closed is never processed (the watch job only warns about it), so it is never pruned either, and one row for each such Job stays. Accepted: the alternative is a table that keeps every window for ever; a longer `sync_window_retention` narrows the first case and grows the table by the same amount, and it is a setting a human can change.
- **Denied files leave traces.** The old versions of a file that became denied stay in the blob store until the retention job collects them, which it does like any other version, once they are beyond the keep rules (D104, D116: not among the last 50 versions of their file and older than 180 days): the blob policy refuses their hashes meanwhile and afterwards (the history names them), but they are stored. `compare_tree` shows the names and hashes of a path that another swimlane's agent flagged as denied, when this swimlane's agent did not. `FileObserved` events and the audit chain name denied paths (D79: the name, the size and the hash are not secret). A comparison of two Git refs is of the repository and not of a swimlane, so a path that only an agent's flag denies is readable there (config in Git is not secret, D18). A denied file's hash is an id that anyone with the right to read the tree sees.
- **The pickup state trusts what the agent reports, and has no per-tenant targeting.** The state of a service (live, live within the TTL, needs notify or restart) is computed from the pod start times and the Deployments' environment that the agent sends and from the config-server start time it sends: a hostile agent can make services look restarted or the config-server look older than it is, and a pod start that is reported wrongly gives a wrong "live". A change to a tenant's file marks the services that read the file, whatever tenant they serve, because the Deployment does not say which tenant it serves. The changes read are the newest 20,000 of a swimlane (`pickup_max_files`), and the metric `registry_pickup_truncated` counts the times there were more. A config-server start that is not known switches C11 off and leaves channels and new folders as ordinary changes.
- **Tool-write registrations are an authority with a time to live (D120).** The same content recreated by someone else inside the window (the TTL, 15 minutes) is indistinguishable from the tool's and is credited to the registering user; for a delete, the same content recreated and deleted again inside the window is credited to the tool's registration, and the assumption that the newest deletion at commit time is the tool's holds only for the length of the registering transaction; the key of a registration has no user, so two registrations of one key from two users keep the first, which is safe only because a request id is made by the hub and nothing enforces that it is unique; concurrent writers of identical content swap between users when the later registration commits first; the bound on pending registrations is soft by the number of registering transactions in flight; a registration the agent never reports (the hub's own writes are applied to the agent's tree) waits for its TTL and can meet identical content written by someone else; and the late-commit rule needs the observation to be applied no earlier than a second before the registration was stamped, which turns an interrupted write that is retried into a false negative (safe: it stays out of band).
- **Attribution has limits (D123).** A compromised sentinel, or root on the VM, can name any application user for any change in its own swimlane within a minute of a record it invents, and can make its swimlane's NFS-client changes `unknown` by staying silent; a local write by user A and an NFS write of the same path within a minute are both A's (a record is not used up); the OS Login to user match trusts the email's form or the admin's map; a reordered sentinel batch is dropped as a resend; a sentinel moved to another swimlane lets its older records explain changes of the second; the heartbeat does not reach the registry, so a VM with nothing written is not "seen" and its NFS writes stay `unknown`; and `sentinel_records` holds paths and OS Login names for up to 90 days.
- **Severity rules are raise-only, can be stale, and some defaults never fire.** An admin's row can raise what a default says and cannot lower it or switch it off (D103), which is a decision for a human; after a rule change a swimlane is rated again at its next batch (its rules fingerprint moved), so a swimlane that nothing changes in keeps its old ratings until the nightly recompute, and they are stale until then; and a default rule that needs `change_kinds` or a `setting_glob` never matches a file's rating (the registry rates a file by its path, state and class), so those defaults do not fire.
- **A lying agent can raise or hide a C12 for its own swimlane.** The check holds the engine's rendering against what an agent says the config-server served. A lying agent can answer with a wrong hash (a false C12) or with the engine's own rendering (hiding a real mismatch) for the combinations of its own swimlane, and for nothing else; the check also cannot tell a config-server that is wrong from an agent that is. It is a smoke alarm for drift between the engine and the config-server, not a proof, and the sentinel and the Git side are the other sources.
- **Property-source merges are not verified as served, and what is verified is a sample.** Only resource files are compared (a property source's effective config is a merge that the config-server's file endpoint does not serve), 200 combinations a night for each swimlane, with the open findings first: a mismatch outside tonight's sample is found when a later night draws it, which for a large swimlane can take many nights (the population divided by 200). A swimlane with an offline agent or an open C11 is skipped and counted. The engine's resolution model is a reconstruction (04's residual risk), and C12 is how a difference becomes visible.
- **The structure checks lag, and a restart forgets them.** C9 to C11 are computed by a sweeper every 10 s after the pipeline's batch; a Deployment's environment names, which no gate announces, are found by the full pass, up to ten minutes later (a test says so); the memory of what a replica last computed is lost at a restart, so the first sweep after one computes every swimlane once. Between a change and its sweep, a list of findings does not yet hold the structure kinds that the change causes.
- **Drift alerts are at most once, and do not cover every state (D98, D100).** The notification for drift that became worth an alert is sent from a task after the commit and at most once: a crash between the commit and the send loses it, and a burst past `alert_in_flight` is dropped and counted. `git_ahead` is not alerted (a change waiting for its sync), and neither is `unknown`, so a file created on NFS and also in Git with other content, which has no baseline, raises no alert; a closed sync window that nobody processed for 24 hours is marked done and nothing is said. Drift is recorded whatever is alerted, and a person who looks sees it.
- **Roles are global, and a viewer reads every swimlane (D35).** Any active viewer reads every swimlane's tree, files, comparisons, findings and the text search over all of them; the registry has no per-swimlane scope. That is the product's design (a few teams, one hub), and the denied files and the redaction are what protect the content that matters.
- **Findings are not audited (D93, rule 8).** Rule 8 says every state change writes exactly one audit event; D93 reads that for the registry as the state changes that are decisions or evidence (adoption, marks, attribution, tenants, deployments, out-of-band changes), and not for findings, derived columns, indexes and caches, which are reproducible from the source facts. A finding that is written, resolved or never written has no audit event; the findings are recomputable, and the audit chain keeps the changes they were computed from.
- **Retention deletes for good, is bounded per run, and is not wired into the hub yet.** The job `registry-retention` (`src/retention`, D116 to D119) is built and tested in this crate and is not registered in `crates/hub` (A8): until the hub registers it, nothing is collected and storage grows. Once it runs it is nightly, it deletes by default (A34; `retention_dry_run` reports and changes nothing) and it takes at most 500 blobs and 64 MiB a run, so a backlog (the first run over a large store, or after a bulk load) is cleared over several nights, and `registry_retention_truncated` says that more is collectable. It never deletes a blob that a reference class or a `blob_refs` row names, one of the last 50 versions of its file or one newer than 180 days, or one stored within the grace period. `file_observations`, resolved findings and the audit chain are not pruned (the keep rules decide only which blobs go), and the sentinel's records are pruned by their own partition job; every question about them is bounded and keyed, so what they cost is disk and backup size and not behaviour. The mode and the numbers are a human's choice (A34), and D116 and D118 have to be confirmed before the job is registered.
- **The audit chain can name a hash whose blob is gone (D116).** An audit event's `hash_before` and `hash_after` do not pin a blob: the chain keeps its own diff text and its hashes (D78), and an event pins a blob only through an explicit `blob_refs` row of kind `audit_event` (`hub-writes` adds one for the blobs of its own edits). A version that an out-of-band or sync event names can therefore be collected once it is beyond the keep rules, and the event then names a hash that the blob store cannot serve (a clean `NotFound`; the diff text in the chain is the evidence). Accepted, because reading the hashes as pins would keep nearly every version for ever so that the keep rules decided nothing, and `audit_events` has no index on them (measured at 500,000 rows: 51 s to check one batch of 500 candidates). What would change it: an index on those columns (a contract migration), or the writers adding `blob_refs` rows for the versions they want kept.
- **A garbage collection is not audited (D118, A12).** Rule 8 asks for one audit event for every state change, and the registry reads it as the decisions and the evidence (D93); a blob is a cache of a version that Git, the chain's own diff text and the history keep. A delete by the job leaves the run's counts and bytes in the log and in the `registry_retention_*` metrics and nothing in the chain, and neither carries a hash (S10 read stricter than it asks), so after the fact nobody can say which blobs a run took. Accepted by A12, which the human approved with the plan. What would change it: a decision that the chain records each run as one event with counts and no hashes, which needs an audit action in `ports` (a contract change).
- **The plan reads every blob on every run, and only its timeout bounds it.** `PLAN_SQL` tests all the blobs older than the grace period for references and keep rules, and there is no index on `blobs.touched_at` (migration 0008 gives none, because every put would pay for it), so the cost is linear in the number of blobs: measured at 300,000 blobs it takes about 2.1 s warm in the worst case. It is bounded by `gc_scan_timeout` (120 s) and by nothing else. If the store grows until the plan times out, nothing is collected, the run fails its `plan` step and the only sign is `registry_retention_failures_total{step="plan"}` and a warning with no driver text: alert on it. What would change it: an index on `touched_at` (a contract migration), or a plan that walks the hashes in ranges.
- **A deleted blob can linger in one replica's `PgBlobStore` cache.** The job deletes from the table and the cache of another replica is not told, so that replica can serve a version that was collected until it evicts it. Harmless: a blob is immutable, so what it serves is still exactly the bytes of that hash, and a read after the eviction is a `NotFound`.
- **Only a `blob_refs` row, or a column that the job reads, pins a blob.** The job reads every column of the schema that can name a blob (`DECISIONS`), and a test fails when a migration adds a column that nobody decided. A writer that references a blob in a way the job cannot see (a hash inside a text column, a version kept only in an audit event, content stored by a `put` and not referenced within the grace period) has its blob collected. Any future writer that references a blob without a `put` must add a `blob_refs` row (the foreign key then protects it) or add a decision to `DECISIONS`; the column test forces that for a column and cannot see into a text field.
- **After a bulk load, the planner takes the tables for empty until autovacuum's first auto-analyze.** After a bulk load of `settings_index` (the first adoption of a large swimlane, a restore) and before the first auto-analyze, the planner takes the tables for nearly empty and chooses plans under which a dense settings search costs 100 to 160 ms a statement instead of 3 to 30 ms (measured at the target scale). `lanekeeper_app` cannot `ANALYZE`, so the registry cannot close the window itself; the bench's read bed now analyses its tables as the owner (commit `f5d5fc7`), which is how a hub in steady state looks. A follow-up contract migration with per-table `autovacuum_analyze_*` settings, or an owner-run `ANALYZE` step in the deploy after a bulk load, would close it. It is not built here: it is a contract follow-up.
- **A read of a collected version is counted, and nothing says which blob it was.** After retention a version whose blob was collected reads as a clean `NotFound` for that version (the history still lists it): the read adds one to `registry_read_blob_missing` and writes at most a debug line, and neither the log nor the metric carries a hash, a path or any content (S10). So the counter cannot tell a collected version from a blob that the store lost: both are the same `NotFound`, and the retention counters (`registry_retention_blobs`) and the store itself are what tell them apart.
- **The blob endpoint refuses what it cannot decide, and remembers a deleted denied file only by its observations.** The blob policy applies the one rule (`is_denied`: the flag, the class, the hub's own `extra_deny_globs`) to every file that has had the content, so a hub that restarted with new globs before its agents reported the files again, and a denied file that was deleted (its row and flag are gone; the newest observation of it that has a class says what it was), are refused as every other read withholds them. Two limits remain. A hash that more than 1,000 observations have had (`HASH_PATHS_LIMIT`; at the design scale the most common content has 49) is refused as undecided, so the endpoint cannot serve content that was copied into that many places (each such request is counted in `registry_blob_withheld_total`, as every refusal is, and writes at most a debug line with no hash, path or content: it is not a warning, because a viewer who knows a popular hash can ask for it at request rate), and a guess that none of them is denied could be the one leak. And the memory of a deleted denied file is its observations: nothing prunes `file_observations` in this build, but a prune that removed the observations of a denied file would make its old versions servable again, so a future prune must keep them or the policy must read another record. A refused hash is a 404, as an unknown one is.
- **An ordinary file's blob is also refused when a denied file has the same bytes** (content addressing: the blob is served only if every path that names it is allowed): a product-visible over-refusal, accepted; a hash with more than 1,000 sightings is refused as undecided. One blob holds one set of bytes whoever names it, so a key that was copied under an ordinary name, or an ordinary file that later got the same bytes as a denied one, is refused as the denied file's blob is: the any-denied rule is right, and this is its visible effect. A viewer gets a 404 for the ordinary file's blob by its hash, and the alternative, serving a blob that a denied file also has, is the leak the endpoint exists to prevent.
- **An agent's times and facts are its own.** An observation's `observed_at` is the agent's, clamped to the hub's clock at the top, so a hostile agent cannot post-date a change, but it can backdate one within the history (an older version is history and never moves the current state back); and everything an agent reports about its own NFS, Deployments and sync windows is believed for its own swimlane, which is why the sentinel and Git are separate sources.
- **Gaps against requirements. These are gaps, not accepted risks, and each needs a decision.** (1) S14b: raw text, blobs, diff hunks and search lines are not masked by this crate (A28, above); the web masks only if prompt 08 does and MCP redacts only if 07 does. (2) S13: the adoption pass scans both repositories, but files over the size limit or that cannot be decoded are `unscanned`, so the pass does not prove them clean. (3) S9 and rule 8 read narrowly (D93, above): findings, derived columns and caches have no audit events. (4) S10: paths and hashes of denied files are in events, the audit chain and `compare_tree` (D79 allows names, sizes and hashes; the old versions' blobs stay until the retention job collects them beyond the keep rules). (5) S4: no rate limit of the registry's own protects the read API from a viewer's repeated expensive requests; the platform's and MCP's limits are the only ones. (6) S25 and S5 for attribution rest on the sentinel and the agent being honest for their own swimlane.

## Write paths and token vault (06)

Owner: prompt 06. This section covers `crates/hub-writes` as built by tasks T1 to T7 and T9 to T11: the write pipeline with its idempotency claims, editing lock, validation and approval step (T1); byte-faithful writes (T2); edit, upload, delete, revert and restart (T3); proposals, approvals, expiry and drafts (T4); the GitHub token vault (T5); the synchronous half of pull requests, which is the reverse mapping, the plan and the Git Data calls (T6); the Teams notifier (T7); pull request links and jobs (T9): the persisted, resumable job that makes a pull request, the one-open-pull-request rule, the approval of a pull request, and the reaction to a pull request that was merged or closed; the reveal action for masked values (T10): the one audited way to see a value the secret scan flags, and the refusal of writes to denied paths at every door; and notify, auto-notify and the restart of the config-server (T11): the one door to the config-server's unauthenticated update endpoint (Q37), the batching of a swimlane's writes into one notification, the pickup states a notification changes, and the restart that check C11 asks for. Sign-in, sessions, CSRF and the audit chain itself are 03a's; the agent's side of a write is 02's; the gateway, the outbox and the leases are 03b's; the registry that observes the result is 05's. This section relies on them and says so where it does.

The module asks two questions. Can someone change a file, a service or a repository without being allowed to, without a record, or against a file that is no longer the one they were shown? And can a user's GitHub token be read by anything but that user's own request? A row's Proof cell names tests, benchmarks or fuzz targets that exist in `crates/hub-writes`; `tests/threat_model.rs` fails if one does not, or if the name is a helper and not a test. A control that is planned but not built has a Proof cell that starts with `None`; the table has none, because what is not built is listed above and in the residual risks.

### Assets

- **The config files on each swimlane's NFS export,** reached only through the agent. A write is a `WriteFile`, `DeleteFile` or `RestartDeployment` command, and the hub never touches NFS itself. The hash the caller expects to find is the precondition of every file command (D44).
- **Content in flight and at rest.** Request bodies (at most 2 MiB of file; the routes allow for JSON escaping), the new version of a proposal (a blob, by hash), a draft (24 hours), and every version a write replaced or created (a blob by hash, D47). Config text is data; it can hold values the scan does not know.
- **Idempotency rows** (`idempotency_keys`). A SHA-256 of the request, never its content, and the stored response. While a write is in flight the row holds its intent: the swimlane, the path, the hashes, and the audit event it will record with a diff in which flagged values are already redacted. Rows live 24 hours.
- **Proposals and approvals.** Who proposed, who decided, and the hash the proposal expects. `lanekeeper_app` can insert an approval and read it, nothing else (migration 0004), and cannot change a proposal's author.
- **The audit events this crate records:** one for each applied write, decision, expiry, token change, restart and notification. They name the actor, and for an approved change the author and the approver (D38).
- **GitHub tokens (S8).** The plaintext exists only inside its owner's request: in the caller's `Secret<String>`, in the cipher's buffer, and in the `Secret` the vault hands to a closure. At rest a row of `github_credentials` holds a fresh AES-256-GCM data key wrapped by Cloud KMS, a nonce and the ciphertext; the associated data is the user id and the credential id. The KMS key's IAM is what stands behind every wrapped key. The linked GitHub login (on the user's row) and the expiry GitHub reports are not secret.
- **Pull request jobs and links** (`pr_jobs`, `pr_links`, T9). A job holds who asked, the paths, the title and the person's text, the state, a failure sentence, and what each step produced: the plan with the *hashes* of its files' blobs (the content is in the blob store, by hash), the branch and commit, the pull request's number and address, and the audit event's id. A link ties a swimlane, a path and an NFS hash to a pull request, and its state (open, merged or closed) comes from GitHub's webhook (03b). Both are bookkeeping that decides whether a second pull request is allowed.
- **The preview key.** The secret `write-preview-key` (at least 32 bytes) from the secret source; with it a preview id is an HMAC-SHA256 token over the user, swimlane, path, action and hashes it was issued for (D46).
- **Flagged values (T10, D79, S14b).** The values the secret scan flags in structured files on NFS. Every view masks them (`[REDACTED: N chars, rule]`); the reveal action is the one path that returns one, to an Editor or above, for a reason, with one audit event. The value exists in the response of the reveal and, briefly, in the memory of the request that reads and parses the file; it is in no table, cache, log, event or error. The audit event keeps the file's hash, the setting, its line and the reason, never the value.
- **The config-server's update endpoint (T11, Q37).** `/update-resources` has no authentication: anything that can reach port 8888 can make every client of a swimlane drop its cache and refresh its context. The hub's only way to it is the `NotifyConfigServer` command to the agent, sent by `ops/notify.rs` and by nothing else; the agent dials its one configured address (02). What the hub protects is who may send the command, with which paths, and that each call is on the record.
- **Pickup states** (`service_state`, `swimlanes.last_notified_at`, T11). What the screens say about whether a service has picked up a change (D85). A notification rewrites them for the services that read the notified paths, in the transaction that records it, so a wrong write here is a wrong fact on every screen and MCP answer that quotes it.
- **The auto-notify queue** (T11). A bounded in-memory queue of written paths, by swimlane and user, between the write path and the batcher. It holds paths and ids, never content, and is lost when the process stops.
- **Teams webhook URLs.** A credential: the query of the URL holds a signature. The settings table names the secret, and the secret source returns the URL.
- **Leases** that serialise a restart of one service and the decision on one proposal across replicas.
- **Events** published through the outbox (`ProposalChanged`, `FileObserved`, `PickupChanged`, `DriftChanged`). They tell the screens to refetch and carry ids, hashes and paths, not content.
- **Availability.** An edit is applied within 1.5 s (P8) and the steady-state queries answer within 50 ms (P12); people lose time, not data, when this slows down, but a restart or an approval that cannot be done is a change that waits.

### Trust boundaries

- **Browser to the write routes.** Every route is registered through `GuardedRouter` with a declared role, behind the platform's limits layer (body cap, JSON depth, timeout, per-IP and per-user rates) and 03a's session and CSRF checks (S3, S4). The body is limited before it is read and read into typed values before the service sees it; the response is a fixed problem that quotes nothing of the request (S11, S21).
- **Browser to the reveal route (T10).** The one route whose success response is a secret. It is guarded like the others (Editor, session, CSRF), takes a small JSON body (16 KiB), and answers `Cache-Control: no-store` whether it succeeds or not. Behind it, the desk asks the directory again for the role, refuses any call that did not come in through the web UI, counts the attempt against the user's window, reads the file through the registry (which withholds a denied file), and commits the audit event before it hands back the value.
- **The service to the user directory.** The `ctx.user` of a request names the user and is trusted for nothing else. The role, the status and the `requires_approval` flag are read from the directory on every action, so a demotion or a disable takes effect on the next call (S4).
- **Hub to agent** (gateway, mutual TLS, 03b). The hub sends the path as a typed `NfsPath` and the expected hash; the agent compares them with the file and refuses on a mismatch, and its own deny globs and cap-std root are 02's. A reply that never arrives is neither a success nor a failure: the write may have run. The hub keeps an intent so that it can find out (the reconcile job), and for a restart it cannot (see the residual risks).
- **Hub to the registry and the blob store.** The registry may be a scan behind, so the file a diff is made against is checked against the expected hash and, failing that, taken from the blob store or the agent. A file the registry withholds (D79) is not read to make a diff. The tool-write registration (05's `ToolWrites`) is made in the transaction that records the write.
- **Hub to Postgres** as `lanekeeper_app`, over verified TLS. The audit log is append-only (01, 03a); approvals are written once and a proposal's author cannot change (migration 0004).
- **Hub to Cloud KMS and Secret Manager.** KMS wraps and unwraps a token's data key under the associated data; Secret Manager supplies the preview key and the Teams URLs. Both are called with a timeout.
- **Hub to `api.github.com` with a user's own token (D41).** HTTPS and TLS 1.3 only, a host allowlist checked at start, no redirect followed, three attempts with full jitter, a cap on the answer, and every answer parsed into a typed value before it is used (S6, S18). The token reaches nothing else.
- **A job to GitHub, later than the request (T9).** A pull request is made by a job after the request that asked for it was answered, and a resumed job may run on another replica. It runs as its author: it opens the author's own token for each GitHub call, after the directory is asked again whether the author is still active and an Editor. Nothing else about the token changes (the same `with_token`, the same host, the same buffers).
- **Hub to the config-server, through the agent (T11, Q37).** The hub sends `NotifyConfigServer` with the paths and nothing else; the agent builds one form value for each path and dials the address it was configured with (02). The endpoint authenticates nobody, so the hub's authorization is the only authorization: Operator, read from the directory on every call, for a person, and an approval for a user who needs one. A status that is not `2xx`, an agent that has no address for the config-server, and an answer that never arrives are three different outcomes, and none of the first two is recorded as a notification.
- **Write path to the auto-notify batcher (T11).** A write that has committed offers its path to a bounded queue; the batcher, a task of the process, makes the calls. The offer never fails or delays the write beyond one indexed read of the swimlane's switch, and runs only after a batcher has been asked for. The batcher is the same code as a person's notify (`notify_as`), so its calls are authorized, claimed, sent and recorded the same way, with `via = system`.
- **Hub to the Teams webhook** (S18). HTTPS, the default port, no user in the URL, a host on the allowlist, no redirect. The card is built from the notification and fixed words only.
- **The engine.** Bytes go in and facts come out, on the blocking pool and at most eight validations at once. The engine's own limits and its residual risks are in its section (04).
- **Container to node and cluster** (S16, S18) and **build to deployment** (S19) are 09's; the crate adds one dependency family, octocrab with hyper-rustls, over the stack's TLS provider.

### STRIDE

| | Threat | Mitigation | Proof | S# |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S | A caller acts with a role they no longer have, as a pending or disabled user, or claims a role, a status or the right to skip approval in the request. | `authorize` reads the user from the directory on every action and compares the role with the floor of the action (Editor for files, drafts and tokens, Operator for restarts, Admin for decisions); `ctx.user` is only an identity. A user who cannot be read is `Unavailable`, not allowed. A pending or disabled user fails before a claim, a lock or a command exists. The vault asks the directory too, so a stale session snapshot does not open a token. | `matrix_every_action_by_role_and_status`, `pending_and_disabled_users_fail_before_any_side_effect`, `the_role_is_the_directorys_and_not_the_one_in_the_request`, `approval_flag_comes_from_the_directory_too`, `a_stale_session_snapshot_does_not_open_a_token` | S4 |
| S | One user replays, or reads the stored answer of, another user's idempotency key. | The key of a claim is the user's tenant id, object id and the header, so two users with the same header text are two claims; the stored response is returned only to the request that has the same user, key and request hash. | `keys_belong_to_the_user_who_sent_them` | S4, S11 |
| S | A user stores a token that belongs to another GitHub account, or later swaps accounts to take another person's authorship on GitHub. | GitHub is asked who the token belongs to. The first save links that login to the user; a later save for another login is refused until an admin resets the link, and the check is repeated under the user's row lock, so two first saves for two accounts link exactly one. A reset is Admin only and audited. | `mismatched_login_is_refused`, `racing_first_saves_for_two_accounts_link_exactly_one`, `the_link_survives_deleting_the_token_and_an_admin_can_reset_it` | S8 |
| T | A write overwrites a change somebody else made after the writer looked (a blind write, a lost update), or a retry applies a change twice. | Every file command carries the hash the caller expects (or "absent") and the agent compares it with the file; a mismatch is a conflict with the current hash and writes nothing. Approving a proposal looks at the file again. The same key and the same request returns the stored answer, the same key with another request is 422, and two requests with one key run once. | `edit_conflict_is_409_with_current_hash_and_writes_nothing`, `upload_conflict_is_409`, `delete_conflict_is_409`, `revert_conflict_is_409`, `approval_on_changed_file_marks_stale_and_writes_nothing`, `same_key_same_request_replays_stored_response_and_writes_once`, `same_key_different_request_is_422_and_writes_nothing`, `concurrent_same_key_executes_once`, `field_boundaries_cannot_collide` | S11, S17 |
| T | A request in a shape the contract does not describe slips past validation: a JSON array read as the struct, an unknown field, a traversing path, a multipart form with a field or a part the contract does not name, an oversized body. | Each request is read by a pure function into `domain` types (`NfsPath`, `ContentHash`, `ServiceRef`, `ShortText`) before the service sees it; a JSON body has to be an object before serde reads it; unknown fields are refused; the multipart reader is strict and linear; a body is limited before it is read; every refusal is one of three fixed words. | `a_json_array_is_not_an_object_for_any_reader`, `a_request_that_is_not_well_formed_never_reaches_the_service`, `a_form_that_is_not_exactly_the_contracts_is_refused`, `bodies_are_limited_before_they_are_read`, `what_the_contract_does_not_name_is_refused`, `writes_request_json`, `writes_upload_multipart`, `writes_preview_id` | S11, S22 |
| T | Content changes on its way to the file in a way the author did not write: line endings, a byte order mark or an encoding rewritten, so that a one-line change looks like a rewrite of the whole file, or a binary file is damaged. | The text is put into the form of the file it replaces (line endings, BOM, encoding) and the style changes only when the request says so; a new file takes the style of its folder; binary and denied content is written untouched. The stored diff and the pull request carry only the changed lines. | `crlf_file_edited_from_lf_input_keeps_crlf`, `stored_diff_contains_only_changed_lines`, `bom_is_preserved`, `change_eol_only_when_explicit`, `binary_content_is_written_untouched`, `prepare_content_preserves_style`, `pr_diff_contains_only_changed_lines` | S11 |
| T | A preview id is forged, or carried from one user, path, action or file version to another, so that an upload or a delete skips the effect confirmation. | The id is an HMAC-SHA256 over the user, swimlane, path, action and hashes, with the issue time, keyed from the secret source; it expires after ten minutes and fails if any bit changes or if the key is missing or short. It is not a capability: the expected hash is still checked by the agent. | `preview_bound_to_user_path_and_hash`, `preview_older_than_10_minutes_is_refused`, `tampered_preview_is_refused`, `upload_and_delete_without_preview_are_refused`, `a_token_is_bound_to_each_part_of_the_request`, `any_bit_flip_fails`, `a_token_made_with_another_key_is_refused`, `a_missing_preview_key_refuses_to_judge_and_issues_nothing` | S4, S11 |
| T | A proposal, an approval or a stored token is altered or moved so that somebody approves their own work, a decision is rewritten, or a ciphertext opens for another owner. | An author cannot decide their own proposal (checked first in code, refused again by the trigger on `approvals`); `lanekeeper_app` can insert an approval and nothing else, and cannot change a proposal's author. Every token row has its own data key and its associated data names the user and the credential id, for the cipher and for the KMS wrap; a changed byte, a moved row or another credential id fails to open. | `author_cannot_approve_own_proposal`, `the_database_refuses_self_approval_and_changes_to_a_decision`, `swapped_associated_data_fails_decryption`, `ciphertext_moved_to_another_user_fails`, `swapped_sealed_columns_between_users_fail_decryption_and_restoring_them_opens`, `the_same_bytes_under_another_credential_id_fail_decryption`, `a_changed_stored_byte_fails_decryption`, `fresh_data_key_per_token` | S4, S8 |
| T | An answer from GitHub is hostile or malformed, or a name that a user chose changes the request the hub makes or the text it publishes (a path in a URL, a branch name, a file name in a pull request table). | Every answer is read only if it is an object within a size cap, into a typed value; a blob must be what it says it is, and a repository file is found through the listings of its directories (never the contents call, which follows a symbolic link and answers with the file it points at), so a link, a directory, a submodule or a name that is a file where a directory is needed is refused and a file is never written over a link; a listing that GitHub cut is not read; paths, branch names, logins and ids have strict shapes and are encoded before they are put in a URL; a name cannot break out of a Markdown table cell. | `a_body_over_the_cap_is_refused`, `a_blob_that_is_not_what_it_says_is_refused`, `something_that_is_not_a_regular_file_in_the_repository_is_refused`, `a_symbolic_link_in_the_repository_is_refused_not_replaced_by_a_file`, `a_path_in_a_url_cannot_change_the_request`, `an_encoded_path_has_no_reserved_character`, `a_file_name_cannot_change_the_request`, `a_name_cannot_break_out_of_a_table_cell`, `writes_github_responses`, `writes_git_data_responses`, `writes_github_token` | S11, S22 |
| R | A change cannot be traced to its author, or an approver denies approving: the record is missing, doubled, or can disagree with what happened. | Exactly one audit event for each applied write, decision, expiry, token change and restart, recorded in the transaction that registers the write, publishes its events and stores the response, so they commit together or not at all. An approved change records the approver as the actor and the author and the proposal as such. A conflict, a rejection or a lock changes nothing and records nothing. | `every_applied_write_has_exactly_one_chained_audit_event`, `edit_writes_nfs_then_commits_audit_registration_event_and_response_together`, `audit_failure_rolls_back_the_registration_the_event_and_the_response`, `approval_event_names_author_and_approver`, `rejecting_records_the_reason_and_one_audit_event_and_writes_nothing`, `saving_a_token_writes_exactly_one_chained_audit_event_without_the_token`, `an_audit_failure_rolls_the_whole_save_back` | S9 |
| R | A write lands on NFS and the hub fails before it records it (a crash, a lost reply, a database outage), so that a change exists with no audit event, or a retry writes it twice. | The intent is kept with the claim before the command goes out. The retry with the same key, or the leased reconcile job for a claim older than 60 s, asks the agent for the file: if it has the hash that was sent, the write is recorded as it would have been, once, however many look; otherwise the claim is cleared. The limits of this are in the residual risks. | `reconcile_completes_a_write_that_landed`, `two_reconcilers_complete_a_write_once`, `reconcile_clears_the_claim_when_the_file_is_not_what_was_sent`, `a_lost_reply_is_resolved_by_the_retry_without_a_second_write`, `a_lost_delete_reply_is_resolved_by_the_retry_without_a_second_delete`, `an_agent_that_never_received_the_command_is_rejected_and_the_retry_runs_again` | S9 |
| I | File content or a secret ends up in a log line, an error, a problem body, an idempotency row, a `Debug` print or a Teams card. | Logs carry paths, hashes and ids only; errors are `Copy` enums or fixed words that quote no input; a parse error and a secret match report a line and a rule and never the text; the idempotency row holds a digest; the intent's `Debug` prints ids; a card is built from the notification and fixed words and escapes any Markdown in a name. | `write_path_logs_hold_paths_hashes_and_ids_only`, `upload_delete_revert_restart_and_preview_log_no_content`, `approvals_drafts_and_expiry_log_no_content`, `the_row_holds_a_hash_of_the_request_and_never_its_content`, `errors_are_copy_and_carry_no_input_text`, `parse_error_blocks_with_the_place_and_never_the_text`, `outcomes_that_are_not_successes_are_problems_with_the_fields_the_contract_names`, `a_card_holds_the_notification_and_fixed_words_and_nothing_else` | S10, S21 |
| I | A secret value is written into a config file, kept in a proposal, a draft, a version or a pull request, or shown in a diff, a preview or the approvers' list. | The value-based scan runs on edit, upload, revert and apply-draft, again at approval and before a pull request, and blocks the save, reporting line numbers and rule names only; a flagged value in the old file is redacted in the audit diff and masked in a preview, a draft and the list of proposals; a version that held a secret is not restored; denied files never reach GitHub. | `secret_in_content_blocks_and_reports_lines_only`, `secret_scan_runs_on_edit_upload_revert_and_apply_draft`, `content_is_checked_again_at_approval`, `a_secret_already_in_the_old_file_is_redacted_in_the_audit_diff`, `preview_masks_a_flagged_value_on_either_side_and_never_shows_it`, `a_secret_in_the_old_file_is_masked_in_the_draft`, `the_list_shows_hunks_newest_first_masks_secrets_and_pages_by_keyset`, `revert_does_not_restore_a_version_that_held_a_secret`, `denied_files_and_secrets_never_reach_github`, `denied_path_is_refused_and_nothing_is_sent` | S13, S14b |
| I | A draft is read by someone who did not make it, or by a caller below Editor, through the author's read of a draft (`WriteService::draft`, which the MCP `apply_change` uses to show what is about to be applied). | The read authorizes the caller against the Editor floor and answers `NotFound` for a draft that is not the caller's, as `apply_draft` does, so another user's draft is as good as none, and an expired or applied draft is still the author's alone. It writes nothing, claims no key and records no audit event. The severity and the effects it returns are worked out at the call from the rules and the Deployments (`assess.rs`) and are not stored; the masked values are the ones the draft already held. | `the_author_reads_their_draft_as_proposed_and_nobody_else_does`, `an_applied_or_expired_draft_is_still_readable_by_its_author` | S4, S13 |
| I | A user's GitHub token is read by someone else, logged, returned, or sent where it should not go. | Decrypted only inside its owner's request (the owner is the request's user; there is no call that takes another user's id), handed to a closure as a `Secret`, and zeroized when the closure's future ends. It is sent in the `Authorization` header to one allowlisted host, with no redirect, and never put in a log, an error, an audit event, a response or an idempotency row (the row keeps a digest). A stored token past its expiry is not opened. | `token_never_appears_in_logs_errors_audit_or_responses`, `the_token_and_the_content_are_in_no_log_and_no_error`, `an_error_never_holds_the_token_or_the_servers_words`, `decrypt_happens_only_inside_the_owners_request`, `plaintext_token_is_zeroized_after_use`, `data_key_is_zeroized_after_wrap`, `data_key_is_zeroized_when_kms_refuses`, `the_token_is_sent_in_the_authorization_header_only`, `a_redirect_is_not_followed_and_the_token_goes_nowhere_else`, `the_production_client_refuses_other_addresses`, `pr_uses_the_callers_own_token`, `bad_token_requests_never_reach_the_vault_and_are_not_echoed`, `an_expired_token_is_not_decrypted` | S8, S10 |

| D | Tables and work grow without a ceiling: idempotency rows, proposals nobody decides, drafts, stored tokens of people who left, scans that read a whole table, a pull request with thousands of files. | Idempotency rows expire after 24 hours and a leased job deletes them in bounded pages; proposals expire after 72 hours and drafts after a day, in batches, and a user has a bounded number of open drafts; the cleanup of credentials of users not seen for 30 days works in bounded pages and stops when the lease is lost; the hot statements are read through their keys, and `EXPLAIN` tests refuse a sequential scan; a pull request has at most 100 files and a bounded amount of content. | `idempotency_rows_expire_after_24_hours`, `expired_proposals_are_marked_by_a_leased_job`, `the_expiry_job_works_in_batches`, `drafts_expire_and_an_expired_draft_cannot_be_applied`, `credentials_of_users_not_seen_for_30_days_are_deleted_with_an_event_each`, `the_cleanup_stops_when_the_lease_is_lost_and_works_in_bounded_pages`, `every_hot_statement_avoids_a_sequential_scan`, `pr_paths_are_capped_at_100`, `a_pull_request_takes_a_bounded_amount_of_content` | S11, S21 |

The one place that sends `NotifyConfigServer` is `ops/notify.rs`. Auto-notify makes its call as the user whose writes it holds, through the same function, and reads that user's role when the window closes: a user demoted in the meantime, or one who needs approval, is not announced, and an Editor's write is never announced (plan A13). | `notify_is_audited_and_requires_operator`, `restart_and_notify_need_operator`, `matrix_every_action_by_role_and_status`, `viewer_gets_403_on_every_write_route`, `auto_notify_is_off_unless_the_swimlane_turns_it_on_and_only_for_an_operators_writes`, `auto_notify_never_runs_for_a_user_who_is_no_longer_an_operator` | S4 |

| I | A problem body, a log line or an event of a notification quotes the request: the paths, or what the config-server answered. | The route answers a fixed problem that quotes nothing, or the contract's result (a count and an audit id). | D | The config-server, the agent or the hub is flooded with notifications: a burst of writes under auto-notify, a batch of thousands of paths, a stuck queue, or a person pressing the button in a loop. | Auto-notify opens a window of two seconds at the first write and makes one call for each swimlane and user when it closes, with at most 100 paths each; the queue between the write path and the batcher holds at most 1,024 written paths (a write that finds it full is applied, and its path is counted and logged as dropped); the batcher holds no more than that, closes its window early when it has them, and makes at most eight calls at once, each inside the agent timeout. A person's notify is at most 100 paths in a body of about 605 KiB, behind the platform's rates, and the agent allows four commands at once (02). | `auto_notify_batches_a_multi_file_write_into_one_call`, `a_full_queue_drops_the_path_and_never_fails_the_write`, `a_notification_names_at_most_a_hundred_paths_and_none_is_lost`, `a_batch_is_full_at_its_limit_and_counts_distinct_paths`, `the_notify_body_is_limited_before_it_is_read_and_100_paths_fit` | S11, S21 |

| E | A user who needs approval, or one below Operator, gets the config-server called anyway: through the approval of a proposal whose paths were altered, an admin approving their own, or an automatic notification of a write that was only a proposal. | `requires_approval` is read from the directory, so the notification becomes a pending proposal and nothing is sent; only an admin who is not the author approves, and the approval sends the proposal's stored paths, each read again as an `NfsPath` and refused unless there are one to 100. | E | The config-server is restarted by someone who may not, when it should not be, or without people knowing which services it takes away. | The offer is Operator only and exists only while check C11 is open for the swimlane and the cluster report lists exactly one Deployment with the configured name (`csp-configuration-server` unless the configuration says otherwise); with none or several the hub offers nothing and does not guess.
