### Residual risks

- **D-1: `/mcp` is a `public` route that authenticates itself and is not behind `Role(Viewer)`. A deviation from plan 07, accepted by the human for now and to be revisited (D109).** Plan 07 registers `/mcp` on a `hub_platform::GuardedRouter` at Viewer. As built (`server/guard.rs`) it is registered as `public`, with the reason in the route table, and the gate in front of rmcp does the work of the guard on every method: Host, Origin, the bearer token, `Users::require_active`, and then the role of each tool. The reason is that `GuardedRouter` turns a failed guard into a plain 401, and OAuth discovery by Cursor starts from a `WWW-Authenticate` header on that 401 which only a layer outside the guard can add. AGENTS.md rule 9 ("a route-table test fails if any route lacks a guard") is therefore met by an explicit `public` allowance and by named tests, and not by a role guard. The route table: `every_mcp_route_has_a_declared_guard`, `the_routes_of_each_mode_are_exactly_these`, `routes_agree_with_openapi_and_allowances`, and in the hub `hub:mcp_routes_pass_the_hub_route_table_check`. Default deny and the gate on every method: `unmatched_path_under_mcp_is_default_deny`, `every_method_of_the_endpoint_is_gated`, `hub:mcp_default_deny_holds`. A caller with no token is refused (`missing_header_is_401_with_resource_metadata_challenge`), as is one with a wrong audience (`wrong_audience_is_401`), a pending user (`pending_user_is_403`), a disabled user (`disabled_user_is_403`) and a revoked personal token (`revoked_pat_is_401_at_once_on_the_same_replica`). The cost is that the guarantee rests on the gate being on every method of the route: a new method or route under `/mcp` that forgot the gate would not be caught by a role guard (the route-table test lists the three methods and their reason, so it would show). Later, a three-line `GuardedRouter::layer` in hub-platform (03b's crate, its own small PR) can put `/mcp` back on `Role(Viewer)`; only `server/guard.rs` would change.
- **D-2: S3 (CSRF) for the personal-token routes is met by the `Origin` check only. A deviation from plan 07, accepted by the human for now and to be revisited (D109).** Plan A11 says S3 holds if the hub applies 03a's CSRF layer to every non-GET route, and that the work stops if it does not. The hub composes no CSRF layer today, so `POST` and `DELETE` on `/api/v1/me/mcp-tokens` refuse a missing or foreign `Origin` themselves, before the body is read (`state_changing_requests_need_the_public_origin`). The double-submit token half of S3 is not implemented in 07, so it has no test and depends on whoever composes 03a's CSRF layer into the hub (the integration PR). The condition recorded in D109: the hub composition wraps every non-GET route, including `/api/v1/me/mcp-tokens`, in `CsrfLayer`, and `crates/hub/tests/mcp_module.rs` then asserts it; until then nothing is exposed, because the hub does not mount `/mcp` or these routes. The residual risk is the loss of defence in depth: the exact `Origin` check is the only control. Browsers send `Origin` on cross-origin POST and DELETE and S2 requires the session cookie to be SameSite=Lax, so a forged cross-site request should fail; a forged create would mint a token whose value comes back only in the response to the browser. The reviewer decides whether this blocks merge.
- **A prompt injection that reaches a write is contained on the server, not prevented (S14, S15).** The containment is the role on every call, an exact-answer elicitation, a question built from server data, expected hashes and preview tokens, `requires_approval`, and the write rate limit. What it cannot stop: a person who approves without reading (a hostile page can make the assistant ask up to 4 questions at once and 12 a minute, and the server cannot make anyone read); an administrator who puts a write tool on Cursor's allowlist, which removes Cursor's own approval prompt for it and leaves the server's question as the only one (the generated allowlist in the administrator's guide holds the 20 read tools and no write tool, and a test holds that, but Cursor's settings are not ours); and a client without elicitation, which only gets a link, so that the person confirms in the web UI and the same reading problem moves there. The model is not bound by "data, not instructions": a poisoned doc can still steer what the assistant reads and what it tells the person.
- **Secrets in output: the scan is heuristic, and its tuning waits for Q20 (S14b).** The engine's value-based scan finds known prefixes, key headers, JWT shapes and long high-entropy strings. A secret in another shape is a false negative and goes out in clear: MCP never reveals a value, but it cannot hide what the scan misses. The six files flagged in Q20 block the go-live of 07; `ScanConfig` is tuned in the engine with those files, and nothing in 07 loosens it.
- **`notify_services` reaches an endpoint with no authentication, and Q37 is open (T7).** The config-server's `/update-resources` takes a call from anyone who can reach it. Q37 asks its owners to restrict it, with a NetworkPolicy, to the dataload Job and the agent, and to approve Lanekeeper calling it. The tool ships switched off (`tools.notify_services.enabled` is `false`; absent from `tools/list` and `forbidden` while it is), and whoever deploys the hub turns it on only after Q37 is answered. When it is on, it is Operator only, asks the person, and goes through the write service and the swimlane's agent, which audit it. None of that protects the endpoint itself: a caller in the cluster that is not Lanekeeper can still call it, and that is outside this crate. What a confirmed call can do is make the services that listen for notifications reload the files it names, a refresh that disturbs them for a moment; it is bounded by the write budget (12 writes a minute, burst 4) and by the person's answer. The question lists the first 3 of up to 100 paths and counts the rest, so a person who approves without reading can approve a long list. This crate checks the paths' shape only (typed, 1 to 100, no duplicates). Auto-notify (D85) is a separate Admin setting: while it is on, the write service notifies after a committed write, so `apply_change`, `upload_file` and `delete_file` also cause a notification, whichever client made the write, and no tool changes the setting.
- **The sweep scans the parts the typed path does not, with the same scan, and the scan is heuristic.** Setting paths, file names, finding messages, service names and doc titles are scanned by the final sweep, which replaces a key-shaped value and counts it. Hashes, commit ids, actor ids, the cursor and the link are not scanned: they are typed values or the server's own, and look like random strings by design. A secret in a shape the scan does not know, or a short password written into a key name or a file name, is shown. Denied files are withheld by the class and the flag the port gives and by the engine's built-in deny globs applied to the name; an extra glob that only the agent's configuration has is known here only through the port's class and flag. `apply_change` takes a draft that the write service made and does not look at the path of the draft: `propose_change` refuses a denied path first, so only a draft made by another route (the web UI) could name one, and the write service refuses to write it (D79).
- **A pull request description is shown in part only (S15).** The question quotes at most the first 240 characters of the description (fewer when the other lines and the link leave less room, none when they leave under 24), cleaned, escaped, scanned for secrets, and with its length. The rest is posted under the user's own GitHub identity without the person having read it: a description of 5,000 characters can say what it likes after the first 240, and a person who approves has seen only the start. The link in the question leads to the swimlane's pull requests, not to the description. A bare address in the quoted start is not defused, because it shows its whole target as text: a client may make it clickable. Whether Cursor renders the question as Markdown is a question for T0 (D91); the escapes hold either way, and in a client that shows plain text they are visible as backslashes. A secret in the description is hidden in the question, which says that one was found; this crate does not refuse such a description, and whether the write service does is not checked here.
- **Personal-token mode is the weaker credential: a long-lived bearer on a laptop.** A token sits in an environment variable (`${env:...}` in the `headers` of `mcp.json`) for up to 7 days, in a shell, a process listing, a backup or a shared screen; whoever has it is that user for MCP until the owner revokes it in the web UI. There is no admin revoke in v1 (disabling the user stops the token on its next call) and no rotation of the HMAC key: replacing the key ends every token. The revocation of a token reaches another replica within the 5 second lookup cache. The mode exists in case OAuth fails on Cursor (T0), and a deployment runs exactly one mode, personal-token mode or OAuth. Its routes carry the S3 gap of D-2.
- **Sessions, open questions and the memory of finished writes live in one replica's memory (A5).** rmcp's stateful sessions, the channel an answer returns on, the table of session owners, the rate buckets and the caches are per replica, and the hub runs three (D62). A request for a session that another replica owns gets 404 and the client starts a new session; an answer that reaches the wrong replica finds no session at the gate and writes nothing, and its question ends by timeout. The design is fail closed, and each failure costs a retry. Availability needs the load balancer to pin `Mcp-Session-Id` to a replica. Plan A5 asks prompt 09 for that affinity, and prompt 09 does not mention it yet (`prompts/09-deployment-ci.md` and `deploy/` hold no such rule), so nobody has accepted the request. Until it is, use draft mode or accept the retries. The rate limits are per replica too, so a user whose requests spread over three replicas has up to three times the budget.
- **The choices of T3 that the plan did not fix.** In draft mode (and for a client without form support, which gets the same answer) the server does not check that the draft exists or is the caller's: `apply_change` answers a link for any draft id and the web UI decides what the link shows. After the answer the gate reads the user again: a disabled user cannot even answer (the answer passes the gate), a lowered role is `forbidden`, and a changed `requires_approval` is `confirmation_cancelled`, because the question said what would happen. A call that asks has a deadline of 185 s (30 s twice, 120 s and 5 s, the sum of its steps), which is longer than the 120 s of the question alone, and it holds one of the user's 8 call slots all that time. The memory of finished writes keeps only successful writes (`applied` and `proposal_created`), so a refused write is asked again; the code returns before it stores a refusal, and `a_refused_write_is_not_stored_in_the_replay_map` pins it for a conflict, a rejection and an error of the write service. Plan 07 had `apply_change` fall back to draft mode for want of a way to read the draft; the contract PR added `WriteService::draft`, and the code now asks with the draft's details.
- **`propose_change` records a draft without asking.** By contract it is Editor-only with `confirmation: none`, because it writes nothing to NFS; it calls `WriteService::propose_draft` with `Via::Mcp`, which creates a record, and the person confirms later in `apply_change`. A hostile assistant of an editor can create drafts at the write rate (12 a minute, burst 4) with up to 2 MiB of content each. The audit of a draft, its expiry and any cap per user belong to 06 and are not checked here.
- **T0 is NOT RUN (D91).** The compatibility test on Cursor 3.22.12, on Windows and on macOS, needs a human with Cursor and the Entra registrations of Q21; every cell of the table in D91 is NOT RUN, and NOT RUN is not a pass. So this is unproven: that Cursor finds Entra's endpoints from our 401 and metadata (and whether the mirror is needed), that Entra accepts the request Cursor builds, that Cursor declares form elicitation and shows our question, that accept, decline, cancel and a timeout complete without a hang, that a write tool off the allowlist always shows Cursor's own prompt, whether the model is shown `structuredContent` (the default `summary` content mode assumes it is; if not, `data_block`), whether Cursor takes an error result that does not match `outputSchema`, what it does on a 404 for a session, and whether it sends `Origin`. If OAuth fails the answer is personal-token mode; if elicitation fails, draft mode; if neither OAuth nor headers work, or elicitation hangs on one system and not the other, the work stops and a human decides. `just mcp-t0-status --strict` is the human's gate before go-live.
- **The Cursor plugin and marketplace format is UNVERIFIED (A9).** cursor.com is not reachable from the build sandbox. The package builder writes `.cursor-plugin/plugin.json`, one `mcp.json` for one mode and the skill; the tests check our own invariants (exactly one of `auth` or `headers`, the skill inside, the allowlist in the administrator's guide equal to the contract's read tools with no write tool), not that Cursor accepts the package. An administrator checks it on the first install.
- **`get_effective_config` is incomplete for resource files, and where the hash of a denied served file comes from is open (D92).** The tool returns `text` as null and every setting with type `other` and no flag (so `yes` and `on` are not flagged), because `domain::EffectiveConfig` carries neither; a later contract PR adds the rendered text and a typed value, together with the open question of where the hash of a denied served file comes from (prompts 04 and 05). `get_served_config` returns the rendered body meanwhile. Secrets are redacted either way.
- **The hub binary does not mount /mcp yet.** `crates/hub` has `McpModule`, and a test runs the route-table check and default deny with it, but `main` does not construct the MCP server: composing it with the real verifier, `Users`, ports and key source belongs to the integration (the hub's `compose` module and prompt 09). A hub built from this branch does not serve `/mcp`. Every control in this section holds for the library as tested with the ports' fakes; none has run in a deployed hub, behind its load balancer, with the real `TokenVerifier`, `Users` and `WriteService`.
- **rmcp logs whole requests at debug, and warns about an allowed origin without a port.** rmcp 3.5.0 logs every request at `debug` (the arguments with it), every result at `debug` (file text with it) and notifications at `info` (S10, D91). The hub's filter holds `rmcp` at `warn` and a hub test asserts it; a binary or a filter that lets rmcp below `warn` would put arguments and file text in the log. Separately, rmcp warns at start for each allowed origin written without a port (it currently matches any port and a future release will match only the scheme default); the public origin is passed that way. Today the gate's exact `Origin` comparison decides first, so rmcp's looser match is a second line only. Before an rmcp bump, write the entries with an explicit port or `:*` and run `foreign_origin_is_refused` and `an_extra_allowed_origin_is_accepted_and_nothing_else`. No test checks the warning itself.
- **Nothing limits callers before authentication.** The per-user limits start after the token is accepted (see the D row). A compromised laptop on the VPN could drive the lookup of made-up personal tokens or the parsing of made-up JWTs; a per-source limit is for the hub's edge, not this crate.
- **A request the gate refuses leaves no line in this crate's records (S10).** Every call that reaches a handler is one log line with the caller's stable user id (`tid:oid`; never the email or the name), whether it was a read, a write or a refusal by role, arguments, rate or tool name, so a read can be attributed even though reads are not audited. A request that the gate refuses (no token, a token that does not verify, a pending or disabled user, a foreign host or origin, an oversized body, a session that is not the caller's) never reaches a handler: it has no line here and no count in the metrics, and for the first kinds there is no user to name. The hub's edge records the request and its status, not who. A pending or disabled user who keeps calling `/mcp` is visible only there. A line for the refusals of a known user is a small follow-up and is not built.
- **Token routes and the hub's conventions.** `Idempotency-Key` is accepted and ignored on the token routes; a hub-level idempotency layer (D68) must not store the response of `POST`, which holds the token. The request id of the token audit events is `mcp-` and random hex made in this crate and not the hub's `x-request-id`, so those events cannot be joined to the hub's request log by id.
- **Some proofs are source scans or scripted.** The gate-only-caller test and the other source rules are text searches (a line that names `writes` and a method), not a proof over the call graph: a call through another variable name would escape them, and the lints and the review stand behind them. The audience and scope tests of this crate script the verifier; the check is hub-identity's. The (pg) proofs need Postgres.
- **Content reaches Cursor's model providers.** See "Known residual risks (from design)": config content returned by tools goes through Cursor to its providers, which is accepted because the files hold no secrets (D18) and the six flagged files are pending review (Q20). Redaction narrows it and does not remove it.
- **The discovery documents are public.** In OAuth mode `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource` (and the optional mirror) are served without authentication and name the tenant id, the Entra authority and the scope. They are static, hold no secret and are reachable only through the VPN-only load balancer.

## Web app (08)

Owner: prompt 08. This section covers `web/` as built by T1 (foundation and S12): the build and the page the hub serves, the Trusted Types policies, the API client and its retry and secret rules, the session in the browser, the lint rules, the editor under the hub's CSP, and the end-to-end CSP run. T3 adds the live-event client and the server-state layer (query keys, writes, optimistic updates). T5 adds masked values, reveal and withheld files, pull-request links, attribution, severity and the four admin sections (sentinels, OS Login mapping, severity rules, retention). T6 adds its rows; a control that is not built yet is marked `None (T#)`. It was written from the code and its tests.

The web app is a single-page application that the hub itself serves (`crates/hub/src/static_files.rs`), under the production policy of `crates/hub/src/headers.rs`: `default-src 'none'`, scripts and styles only with the response's nonce, `require-trusted-types-for 'script'`, two Trusted Types policy names (`lanekeeper-docs`, `lanekeeper-monaco`), no `unsafe-*`, nothing from another origin. The browser holds the user's session cookie (HttpOnly), a readable CSRF cookie and whatever the API returned; the server enforces every permission, so what a person can do in the browser is not what they are allowed to do. The questions are: can text from a file, a doc, a name or an API answer become script, markup or a request the person did not make; can anything the browser holds outlive the person or the page; and can the app be made to call something that is not the hub.

### Assets

- The signed-in person's session (cookie, HttpOnly, `__Host-lk_session`) and the CSRF token (cookie `__Host-lk_csrf`, readable by script by design, S3).
- Secrets that pass through the page: a GitHub token the person types, the one-time value of a personal MCP token or an agent join token, a webhook URL, a value that `revealValue` returns.
- What the API returns: file text, doc text and names (untrusted: they come from NFS, Git and people), role and identity.
- The integrity of what is shown: a path, a name or a diff line that reads differently from what it is.

### Trust boundaries

- Hub to browser: the page, its assets and the API answers. Everything in an answer that came from NFS, Git or a person is untrusted data.
- Page to hub: `fetch` through one module, `src/api/client.ts`, with the cookie, the CSRF header and the real `Origin`.
- Page to the editor chunk: Monaco is 4 MB of third-party code that builds HTML strings and style attributes; it is loaded only on demand and runs under the same policy.
- The build to the image: the image build copies `web/` only and runs `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile && pnpm build`, which includes the dist scan.

### STRIDE

| | Threat | Mitigation | Proof | S# |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S | A page of another origin makes the browser send a write to the hub with the person's cookie. | Every write is a `fetch` with `mode: 'cors'`, so the browser sends the real `Origin` (a `<form method=post>` navigation sends `Origin: null` under `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, which the hub's CSRF layer refuses, so the lint bans `<form method>` and every non-GET is a fetch); the client sends `X-CSRF-Token` read from the cookie on every non-GET and refuses to send to anything but `/api/v1/` and `/auth/`. The hub's CSRF layer is the control; this is the client half, and the hub does not compose the layer yet (D109 D-2). | `client.test.ts` (every write operation of `api/openapi.yaml` through its generated function: CSRF and Idempotency-Key present, GET carries neither; the fetch settings), `lint/rules.test.mjs` (`form-submit`), `e2e/csp/requests.spec.ts` (a real browser's Origin accepted, a write without the token refused by the harness's copy of the rules) | S3 |
| S | A person is shown as signed in as another, or the previous user's data is shown to the next on a shared machine. | A 401 empties the query cache and the ETag cache before it navigates; so does sign-out, which is a `POST /auth/logout` and does not navigate until the hub confirms; the query client has no persister and the only web storage is layout preferences of a typed whitelist. | `auth/session.test.tsx`, `auth/logout.test.tsx`, `lib/query/no-persist.test.ts`, `lib/prefs/prefs.test.ts`, `lint/rules.test.mjs` (`web-storage`) | S2, S10 |
| S | A sign-in link is made to send the person to another site after they sign in (open redirect). | `next` is a same-origin path of plain URL characters, at most 512, with no `//`, no dot segment, no encoded slash or backslash, and never a sign-in or access page; it is checked where it is read from the URL and again when the link is built, and the hub validates `return_to` itself. The sign-in is an `<a href>`, never a script navigation. | `auth/params.test.ts` (property test over 50,000 inputs: whatever is accepted stays on the origin, decoded or not), `auth/session.test.tsx` | S2 |
| T | Text from a file, a doc, a name or an API answer becomes markup or script in the page (XSS). | There is no sink that takes a string: `innerHTML`, `outerHTML`, `insertAdjacentHTML`, `document.write`, `DOMParser`, `srcdoc`, `eval`, `new Function`, string timers and `javascript:` URLs are lint errors with the reason in the message, and Trusted Types makes the browser refuse them as well. The one exception is `RenderedDoc.html`, which the server sanitised, through `SanitisedDocHtml`: the only component that sets inner HTML, the only importer of the `lanekeeper-docs` policy, and it takes only the generated `RenderedDoc` type. `noInlineConfig` stops an `eslint-disable` inside `src`. | `lint/rules.test.mjs` (a bad snippet for every rule id), `lib/csp/sink-access.test.ts`, `SanitisedDocHtml.test.tsx`, `dist-check` (no `eval`, `new Function` or off-site reference in any built file), `e2e/csp/*.spec.ts` (every route and every overlay under the production header: zero violations) | S12 |
| T | A path parameter or a link changes which operation a request reaches: the generated client puts path parameters into the URL as they are. | The router checks each path parameter against the contract's pattern (swimlane id, numeric id, doc slug, admin section) before a screen renders, and the client refuses any URL that is not a plain `/api/v1/` or `/auth/` path (no `..`, no empty segment, no encoded slash, dot or backslash). A doc link is handed to the browser as the server sent it and is never decoded (docs/interfaces.md, open item 6). | `routes/table.test.ts`, `client.test.ts` ("sends only to the hub's own paths"), `auth/session.test.tsx` | S11, S12 |
| T | A search parameter is used as structured data or reaches a place it should not: `?q=123` as a number, `?__proto__=`, `?x={...}`. | The router reads search parameters as strings only, drops every key no route names, bounds the count and the length, and can never set a key of `Object.prototype`. | `routes/search.test.ts` (property round trip), `auth/session.test.tsx` | S11 |
| T | A response is replayed, or a stale or hostile answer is kept: the ETag cache. | The cache is bounded (200 entries, 8 MiB), keyed by URL, emptied on a 401 and on sign-out, keeps the body as text so each caller gets its own copy, never stores a write, a binary answer or a path that returns a secret, and a 304 with nothing kept is an error. | `etag.test.ts`, `client.test.ts` | S10 |
| R | A person denies a write the page made. | The page adds no audit of its own: every write carries the CSRF token and an `Idempotency-Key` (a fresh UUID per user attempt, the same key only for the one automatic retry after a network error) and the hub writes the one audit event (rule 8). A write is never retried by the query layer, and never optimistic except the one registry entry of `src/lib/query/optimistic.ts` (see the optimistic-update row). | `client.test.ts`, `lib/query/retry.test.ts`, `lint/rules.test.mjs` (`optimistic-update`) | S9 |
| I | A secret the person types or the API returns stays in the page: in the query cache, the mutation cache, the URL, the console, an error object or storage. | A secret-bearing mutation (GitHub token, personal MCP token, join token, webhook URL, reveal) uses `useSecretMutation`: no retry, `gcTime: 0`, removed from the mutation cache and reset on every outcome and on unmount, and the secret lives only in component state. The client never retries those paths (a retry is a second send or a second audited reveal) and never keeps their answers for revalidation. An `ApiProblem` holds the response only, never the request, so a token in a body cannot reach an error screen. `no-console`. | `lib/query/secret-mutation.test.tsx` (the string is searched for in both caches after success, failure and unmount), `client.test.ts` ("does not put what it sent into the error", the retry and cache deny lists checked against the contract's paths) | S7, S8, S21 |
| I | Hidden values are shown: a flagged value in file or doc text. | M1: the hub masks, `masks` ranges and the marker come from it, and the web never masks by itself; edit mode is off while `masks` is not empty. The web **fails closed** on a mask it cannot trust, and a revealed value follows strict state rules: see "Masked values, reveal and withheld files (T5)" below. The servers do not mask yet (decision D128): until they do the web shows what it is sent, and says nothing that implies it masks. | `components/masking/plan.test.ts`, `features/masking/masking.dom.test.tsx`, `features/file/view/ContentView.test.tsx` ("masks that cannot be trusted never draw the raw text"), `features/masking/RevealableMaskedText.test.tsx` | S14b |
| I | The API leaks through an error screen: a message, a `detail`, an internal path. | An error screen shows the problem's title and request id only; `detail` is shown by a screen only for a code it knows; the message of an error that is not an `ApiProblem` is never shown. | `ErrorScreen` in `routes/screens.tsx`; `ErrorState.test.tsx` (T4) | S21 |
| I | Source maps, comments or the build expose the source in production. | `sourcemap` is off in the production build (`LK_SOURCEMAP=1` is for local debugging), and `dist-check` fails a production dist that has a `.map` file. | `dist-check.test.mjs` | S12 |
| D | The page is made to flood the hub, or hangs on a request. | Every request has a timeout (30 s) and a size cap on the answer (64 MiB, an error body is read at most 64 KiB of); queries retry only a network error, a timeout or a 502, 503 or 504, twice, with full jitter; a 4xx is never retried. | `client.test.ts`, `lib/query/retry.test.ts` | rule 5 |
| D | SSE: one tab opens many streams, or a reconnect storm. | One stream per tab: the provider is mounted once at the router root and opens it only for a signed-in user with a role, closes it before it opens another (a role change reconnects, it does not add), and the engine is built once, from a lazily loaded chunk, so React's development double effect (mount, unmount, mount) cannot make two. A stall watchdog drops a stream that sends no byte for 45 s. Reconnects use bounded exponential backoff with full jitter (1, 2, 4, 8, then 15 s, never under 200 ms), `Retry-After` and the hub's `retry:` hint are a floor (at most 60 s), three failures in a row make the stream `offline` (attempts go on at the cap) and a backoff that has not seen 30 s of healthy stream does not start over. The hub allows 5 streams per user and answers 429 beyond. | `live/sse-client.test.ts` ("never has two streams open", the ladder, 429 and `Retry-After`, the stall), `live/backoff.test.ts`, `live/LiveProvider.test.tsx` (React's double effect under StrictMode, role change, a different user), `live/single-stream.test.tsx` (the whole app: no stream before sign-in, none for a user with no role, one across navigation, closed on sign-out), `e2e/live/live.spec.ts`, `e2e/harness/harness.spec.ts` (the harness answers 429 as the hub does) | P13, rule 5 |
| T | A forged or damaged frame on the stream (a proxy, a compromised hub, a hub bug) makes the page refetch without end, run a huge loop, or act on a field it did not check. | A frame is trusted only when its `event:` equals the `type` of its JSON, the kind is one the contract has, each field has the contract's JSON type and a list has at most 1,000 items; anything else is dropped (and the resume point still moves, because the hub will not replay it). An event is only ever compared with the parts of query keys: it is never rendered, never a URL, never a path to a file. The parser refuses a frame over 64 KiB or a line that never ends and drops the connection. Events are coalesced over 100 ms and deduplicated, a window that names more than 200 distinct things becomes one refetch of what is on screen, and a handler that throws does not stop the stream. So the worst a forged event does is one bounded refetch of queries the user already has open. | `live/events.test.ts` (every kind against the schema, a wrong type, a list over 1,000, an unknown kind, `__proto__`), `live/sse-parser.test.ts` (the caps, 10,000 random streams cut anywhere), `live/sse-client.test.ts` ("drops a frame it cannot trust", "survives a handler that throws"), `live/invalidation.test.ts` (coalescing, the 200-target fallback) | S11, rule 5 |
| S | A 401 or 403 on the stream is mishandled: the app keeps asking, or keeps the previous user's data. | The stream cannot use the fetch layer (it never ends), so it hands every failed response to the same `signalSessionProblem` that `apiFetch` uses: a 401 empties the query and ETag caches and goes to sign-in, a 403 for a user with no role or a disabled one goes to its page. Then the stream stops and is not opened again (a 401, a 403 and a 400 are not retried). A different user, or a different role, is never resumed from the previous one's id. | `live/sse-client.test.ts` ("the answers that end it"), `live/LiveProvider.test.tsx` ("does not open a stream again after a 401", "starts from nothing when the user is a different one"), `live/single-stream.test.tsx` | S2, S4 |
| T | An optimistic update shows a result that the server then refuses or has not decided (a conflict, an approval, a lock), or writes a secret into the cache. | `onMutate` and `setQueryData` are lint errors everywhere in `src/` but one file, `src/lib/query/optimistic.ts`, a named override that lifts that one rule and no other. The helper serves a closed registry (today: the auto-notify switch of a swimlane, a PUT of the whole value that the contract gives no 202, 409, 412 or 423 and no expected hash) and a test reads `api/openapi.yaml` to check every entry and to prove that every write that can answer 202, 409, 412 or 423, every secret-bearing write and the reveal are not on it. The helper cancels what is loading, rolls back on failure, never invents data for an entry that holds none, and always refetches on settle. | `lint/rules.test.mjs` ("optimistic updates: the ban fires everywhere, and one file is exempt"), `lib/query/optimistic.test.ts` | S14b, S7 |
| T | A write leaves the screen showing old data, or an invalidation refetches far more than the write changed. | The table of what each write invalidates is typed by the generated operation list (a mutating operation without a row does not compile), is applied through the same targets as the live events, and says `none` with a reason for the operations that change nothing the app shows. A write that was only sent for approval refetches the queue and not the file. After a failure the cache is refetched only when it is probably out of date. No call invalidates without a filter (lint and a source scan). | `lib/query/invalidate.test.ts`, `lib/query/write.test.ts`, `lib/query/keys-usage.test.ts`, `lint/rules.test.mjs` | P13 |
| I | Server data is copied into a second store that a live event cannot refresh (so a screen shows data the hub has since changed or taken away), or into storage. | The QueryClient is the one store of server data. No client-state library may be imported (lint) or be a dependency, direct or transitive (a test reads `package.json` and the lockfile); there is no query persister and web storage is for layout preferences only; no context or module-level variable is typed with a type of the generated API apart from two reviewed constants and the session projection of the `me` query. The scan reads declared types one file at a time and says that it is an approximation. | `lib/query/server-state.test.ts`, `lib/query/no-persist.test.ts`, `lint/restricted-imports.test.mjs` | S2, S10 |
| E | A person acts above their role by using the UI: a hidden button, a direct URL. | The server enforces every permission; the UI explains. A route has a minimum role (`src/routes/table.ts`), a role too low shows a permission screen, a control is disabled with a tooltip naming the role (`GatedButton`, `useWriteGate`). Nothing here is a control. | `auth/session.test.tsx` (the role gate), `src/api/roles.test.ts` (every operation has a role), `GatedButton.test.tsx` | S4 |
| E | A new dependency or a build step brings in code that sends data out or runs at install. | Every direct dependency is pinned exactly and at least 14 days old; `pnpm` refuses a release under 14 days (`minimumReleaseAge`) for the whole tree; install scripts are blocked; `dist-check` fails a built file that names a CDN or analytics host, makes an off-site request, or contains `eval`; `osv-scanner` and `pnpm audit` run in CI. | `scripts/dist-check.test.mjs`, `pnpm audit` (no known vulnerabilities at the T1 commit) | S19 |

### The editor under the CSP (S12-M)

Monaco 0.57.0 cannot run under this policy as shipped: it creates 11 or more Trusted Types policies (the hub allows two names), builds `<style>` elements without a nonce, and builds line and overlay HTML with inline `style="..."` attributes, which the policy refuses (no `style-src-attr`, and a nonce does not cover an attribute). The measurement (G0, `plans/08-frontend.blockers.md`) showed 171 style-attribute reports at load and thousands during use, with overlay boxes that lose their size and lines that lose their position; with the rewrite switched off, `e2e/editor/csp.spec.ts` reports 1,370 in its first test. The human chose to keep the policy as it is and adapt Monaco (option (e) of W3), in these parts, all in `web/src/editor/`:

- **One policy for every name.** `MonacoEnvironment.createTrustedTypesPolicy` returns the single `lanekeeper-monaco` policy for every name Monaco asks for. Its `createHTML` renames the `style` attribute of every tag in the string to a data attribute whose name is random per page load (the rewrite is tag-aware: text between tags is never touched); its `createScriptURL` accepts only a same-origin `/assets/<name>.js`. `environment.ts` sets this before any Monaco module is evaluated, because Monaco makes its policies at module evaluation.
- **Three patches of built-ins** (`global-patches.ts`, the lint exception `monaco-global-patches`, which also is the only place the `global-patch` rule is lifted): a nonce on `<style>` elements made with `createElement`; `setAttribute('style', v)` written to `el.style.cssText` (Monaco's diff editor calls it once); and a MutationObserver that copies the renamed style data into `el.style`, which is CSSOM and allowed. They are installed when the editor chunk loads and never before.
- **Markdown-rendering contributions are not in the entry** (`entry.ts`): hover, suggest, parameter hints, inline completions, the colour picker, links, code lens, inlay hints and the go-to-symbol peek. Their renderer asks for a Trusted Types policy named `dompurify` and parses with `DOMParser`, both of which the policy refuses. Turning their options off does not remove the commands, so the modules are left out. The vendored DOMPurify never runs.
- **The worker** is the one built script, `/assets/editor.worker-<hash>.js`, made through the policy; it runs under the API policy (`default-src 'none'`).

What an attacker gains from the patches: nothing unless they already run script or inject HTML on the page. The nonce goes on `<style>` elements that script creates (script is already trusted); `setAttribute('style')` is as powerful as `el.style.cssText`, which any script can do; and the observer acts only on an attribute with a name that is random per page load, so HTML that arrives another way (the docs HTML, whose allowlist has no `data-` attributes) cannot carry it. The patches change the behaviour of the whole page while the editor is loaded.

| | Threat | Mitigation | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| T | A Monaco upgrade adds a contribution that renders Markdown, writes a style attribute another way, or asks for another policy, and the editor breaks or the policy is loosened to fix it. | Monaco is pinned to 0.57.0. `e2e/editor/csp.spec.ts` repeats the G0 stages (load, typing, selection, a 4,000-line scroll sweep, find, palette, wrap, fold, multiple cursors, diff in both modes, five languages, Unicode highlighting, model text that contains `style="..."`, hover and suggest attempts) under the production header and fails on any violation report, any policy other than `lanekeeper-monaco`, any line more than 2 px from its place, and any style data left unapplied. It is the gate for every upgrade. | `e2e/editor/csp.spec.ts`, `editor/editor.test.ts`, `editor/environment.test.ts`, `scripts/editor-structure.test.mjs` |
| T | The editor's text is changed by the rewrite: model text containing `style="..."` shown differently. | The rewrite touches attributes inside tags only (the first version, a regular expression over the whole string, changed 4 of 5 test lines). | `editor.test.ts` (property test), `e2e/editor/csp.spec.ts` ("shows model text that contains style" exactly as it is) |
| I | A hidden character makes a value or a name read differently from what it is. | Unicode highlighting stays on for invisible and ambiguous characters, and control characters are rendered (S8); `SafeText` does the same outside the editor. | `e2e/editor/csp.spec.ts` ("marks invisible and ambiguous characters") |

### Copying a one-time secret, and copying in general (T4 Phase C1b, decision 11)

The human asked on 2026-10-17 for a copy button on the secrets that are shown once (the admin join token and a personal MCP token) and for a consistent, unobtrusive copy experience everywhere. That reverses the earlier choice not to put a secret on the clipboard from the page, so it is recorded here with what it costs.

- **What was built.** `SecretCopyButton` (`components/app/secret/`), imported only by the two displays of a one-time secret (`PersonalTokens`, `JoinTokenCard`; a lint rule and `secret-imports.test.ts` say so), and the generic `CopyButton`, which has no `secret` prop and renders nothing if one is passed. Both go through one function, `writeClipboard`, from a click handler. The GitHub token a person types and a value that `revealValue` returns are never copied by the page.
- **What stays true.** The token is shown once and is revocable on the MCP page (a join token expires in 24 h and works for one agent). The page keeps the token in component state only, as before; the copy button takes it from a getter at the moment of the click and keeps nothing: not in state, a key, a data attribute, a title, the accessible name, the query or mutation cache, the console or a toast. What it says ("Copied. It will not be shown again.") never holds the value. It goes with the secret when the secret is dismissed or the display unmounts, and its timer with it.

| | Threat | Mitigation | Proof | S# |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| I | Another application, or a clipboard-history manager, reads the token after the write. | **Residual.** Nothing in a web page can prevent it, and the page cannot take the token back out: writing an empty string later needs the document focused and could overwrite something else the person copied since. Reduced by: the token is shown once, is revocable (MCP page) or expires in 24 h (join token), and works for one purpose (an agent in one swimlane; an MCP client for seven days). The person is told it will not be shown again, so they store it, and `I have stored it` removes the field. | `secret/SecretCopyButton.test.tsx`; the end-to-end check in `e2e/csp/clipboard.spec.ts` | S7, S21 |
| I | A script or an injected element writes the clipboard without the person (copy on page load). | A click is the only thing that calls the write; nothing runs on mount, render or a timer, and with no user activation (`navigator.userActivation.isActive` false) the button does not try, which is also what the browser requires. The value is read by `getValue()` inside the click. | `SecretCopyButton.test.tsx` ("nothing is read or written until the person clicks", "writes only for a click the person made") | S12 |
| I | The token is kept somewhere the page can leak it. | `getValue` is a function, not a prop; the token is not in state, a React key, an attribute, a title, the name of the button or the words it says; the tests scan the DOM, the QueryClient, the MutationCache, the console and the toaster after a copy, after the secret is dismissed and after unmount. The button never logs (lint `no-console`, and a test spies on every console method on success and on failure). | `SecretCopyButton.test.tsx`, `PersonalTokens.test.tsx`, `SwimlanesAdminPage.test.tsx` | S21 |
| I | A masked value is copied as if it were the value. | An empty value or a text with the redaction marker is refused ("Nothing to copy."), and a masked setting value gets no copy button at all (`isMaskedValue`). The one-time displays never hold a masked value; the check is the guard. | `SecretCopyButton.test.tsx`, `copy-text.test.ts`, `StatusPage.test.tsx` | S14b |
| T | Copied text is not what the page showed: a path or a name with a line break runs the next line when pasted into a terminal, or with a bidi or zero-width character reads differently from what it is. | A single-line value that holds a line break or any control character is not copied ("Not copied: it contains a line break or control character."); text (a draft, the support details) may hold line breaks and nothing else; a hidden character is copied as it is, because the value really holds it, and the confirmation says "Copied. It contains hidden characters." while `SafeText` shows each as `<U+XXXX>`. | `copy-text.test.ts`, `CopyButton.test.tsx` | S8 |
| T | A copied link goes to another site, or carries a script. | A link is made from the route table (`linkTo`) and this page's origin, and only for an absolute path of the app: another origin, `//host`, a scheme, a backslash, a space or a control character is refused; a fragment is set through `URL`, which encodes it. | `copy-link.test.tsx` | S11, S12 |
| I | "Copy details for support" leaks what it should not. | It is built from the `ApiProblem` alone (the response, never the request): the request ID, the problem's title and type, the status and the time. The server's `detail` and the problem's extension members are left out, and a title is made one line. | `support-details.test.ts`, `states.test.tsx` | S21 |
| D | The clipboard is blocked (an insecure page, a Permissions-Policy, a browser setting). | The button says so ("Couldn't copy. Select the text and copy it manually.") and selects the text (the token field for the secret button), so the person can copy by hand. The hub's header leaves `clipboard-write` at its default (`self`): measured in Chromium 141 under the real header, the secret copy puts the token on the clipboard, and with `clipboard-write=()` added the browser refuses and the button degrades as above. | `e2e/csp/clipboard.spec.ts` | S12 |

### Masked values, reveal and withheld files (T5)

The server replaces a flagged value with the marker `[REDACTED: N chars, rule]` and lists where each marker sits (`FileContent`, `ServedFile` and `DocText` carry `masks`, UTF-8 byte offsets into the `text` of the same object). The web never masks anything itself (rule 7) and never has the value; what it must get right is that a mask it cannot check never turns into raw text on the screen, and that a value it is given on request lives as short as it can. `components/masking/plan.ts` turns the offsets into places in each line; `MaskedTextView` draws them; `features/masking` has the Reveal control.

- **Fail closed.** A mask that can be placed on a line but not checked (its span is not exactly a marker, it cuts a character, it overlaps another, the line is too long to convert) hides **the whole line**: nothing of the line is drawn as text and the line says "Hidden line". A mask that cannot be placed at all (not a whole number, a line the text does not have, a range that leaves its line, more than 1,000) **refuses the whole text** with "This text can't be shown safely". A conforming server never causes either. The first file view built in T4 dropped such a mask and drew the raw line (a defect found at T5 integration: a test that planted a raw value under a mask showed it in the DOM); it was replaced, and the same test now passes against the fail-closed view.
- **Reveal.** `revealValue` (Editor and above, audited by the server, 30 a minute) goes through `useSecretMutation` only: `gcTime: 0`, removed and reset on every outcome, never retried (a retry is a second audited reveal). The dialog asks for a reason and says the reveal is recorded. The value lives in the state of one component and nowhere else: not the query cache, the mutation cache, storage, a URL, a log, a toast, an attribute or an accessible name, and it is never copied (this page has no copy for it). It is cleared after 30 seconds, when the person presses Hide, when the window loses focus or the tab is hidden, when the file or its text changes, when another value is revealed (one at a time), on unmount, and on every outcome that is not a success; an answer that arrives after any of that is dropped and the request is aborted. A very long value is clipped at 2,000 characters and says so; hidden characters in it are shown as markers.
- **What the token says.** The hidden-value token holds only fixed words, the length the marker states, and (to a screen reader) the setting's name; the live region that announces a reveal never holds the value.
- **Withheld files.** A file of the denied class, or whose content the hub withholds, shows its name, size, hash and who changed it, and nothing else: no text, no download, no edit, no upload, no compare, and no copy button on the hash. The banner says "sensitive file type" only for the denied class; for any other file whose content is withheld it says the hub could not check it for secrets (the contract fails closed).

| | Threat | Mitigation | Proof | S# |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| I | A mask the hub got wrong (the span holds the value, the offsets are off, the line is missing) makes the web draw the raw text. | Fail closed as above: the line is hidden, or the text refused; the web never guesses what was meant. A property test checks that a span covered by a mask never comes out as text. | `components/masking/plan.test.ts` (including the property test), `masking.dom.test.tsx` ("a hub that breaks the contract"), `features/file/view/ContentView.test.tsx` | S14b |
| I | A revealed value outlives the moment it was asked for. | The state rules above; the timer and the blur and visibility hooks are cleared on unmount; an answer that arrives late is dropped. | `RevealableMaskedText.test.tsx` ("hides the value on its own after the time is up", "when the window loses focus", "one value at a time", "clears the value when the text it was revealed in changes", "drops an answer that comes back for a text that has since changed", "leaves nothing behind when it unmounts") | S14b, S21 |
| I | The value is kept by the data layer or the console. | `useSecretMutation`; the tests scan the QueryClient and the MutationCache for the planted value after a reveal, after Hide and after a failure, and spy on every console method. | `masking.dom.test.tsx` ("the planted secret is reachable by one audited request, and shows only where a test says it may"), `RevealableMaskedText.test.tsx` | S21 |
| R | A person reveals a value and the record is lost, or the answer is. | The server writes the audit record. A failure that may have been recorded (a 500, a lost connection, a timeout) says so, and says that trying again is another record; nothing is sent twice by itself; a second press while one is in flight is ignored. | `reveal-error.test.ts`, `RevealableMaskedText.test.tsx` ("a reveal that fails") | S9 |
| D | Reveals are used to flood the hub. | The server limits them (30 a minute, 429). The page shows the wait from `Retry-After`, sends exactly one request for each press, and never asks again by itself. | `RevealableMaskedText.test.tsx` ("429: says how long to wait, once, and never asks again by itself") | rule 5 |
| E | A Viewer reveals a value through the UI. | The Reveal button is disabled with the role it needs, and is off while the live connection is down. The server enforces the role (`revealValue` is Editor) whatever the page shows. | `RevealableMaskedText.test.tsx` ("disables Reveal for a Viewer") | S4 |
| I | A denied file's name, hash or attribution is used to reach its content. | There is no content to reach: the content answer carries no text, and the page draws no edit, upload, delete, download, compare or copy for the file (nor a link to its blob), and no copy button on its hash. | `features/file/view/ContentView.test.tsx` ("a file whose content is withheld"), `FilePage.test.tsx` | S14b |

### Pull requests, attribution, severity and the admin sections of T5

- **A pull request address is the server's, which got it from GitHub: untrusted.** `components/pr/pr-url.ts` makes a link of it only when it is an https address with a host, no credentials and no whitespace, control or backslash; what is drawn, opened and copied is the one canonical string; the link opens GitHub in another tab with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`. A 409 `duplicate_pr` carries a link that is parsed member by member (`parsePrLink`) before anything is drawn; a malformed one is an ordinary conflict. A job's state is the only source of its progress and "created" is said only for a completed job. Proof: `pr-url.test.ts`, `duplicate.test.ts`, `job-steps.test.ts`, `PrBadge.test.tsx`, `PrJobView.test.tsx`. S12, S11.
- **Attribution text comes from an NFS VM, a cluster or a person: untrusted.** An OS login, a job name and an executable go through `SafeText` (bidi controls, zero-width and other invisible characters show as markers). A person is named only by the user list an Admin has; anyone else sees "Lanekeeper user" and the start of the id, and the web never makes a name up. The source `unknown` carries `low` in the contract as a placeholder and is shown with no score, so a weak guess is never presented as a measured level. Proof: `components/attribution/*.test.ts(x)`, `features/attribution/*.test.ts(x)`. S12, S25.
- **Severity is display only.** The registry rates (the engine's defaults and the admin's rules); the web sorts and filters what is loaded, and a filter on a list with no server-side filter says it applies to the rows loaded so far. Proof: `components/attribution/severity.test.ts`, `Tabs.test.tsx` (the Drift tab filter). Rule 7.
- **Sentinels, OS Login mapping, severity rules and retention are Admin sections;** the server enforces the role. The sentinel list is read-only and says "Last seen", not a heartbeat (D123 records only the hello and the record batches). The OS Login map deletes an entry by its name in the path, percent-encoded; the API client refuses `%2f` and `..`, so an entry whose name contains a slash (it cannot be created by the UI, which accepts a plain login name only) cannot be deleted from the page. Severity rules are raise-only (D103): the page has no control that lowers a built-in rule. Proof: `features/admin/sections/*.test.tsx`.
- **Retention deletes by default (D118) and the page cannot show the job's own dry-run switch.** The contract exposes the policy (`keep_days`, `keep_last_versions`) and a `dry_run` preview of one policy, but not the hub setting `retention_dry_run` that decides whether the nightly job deletes at all. The page therefore says plainly that the job deletes and that a deleted version cannot be restored, requires a dry run for the exact values before Save is on, and asks for a typed confirmation (`delete versions`) before it saves a policy whose dry run finds versions to delete. It cannot tell the person that the job is, today, only looking; read every policy as deleting. **Proposed contract follow-up (for a human, a `contract-change` PR):** `job_dry_run` on `RetentionSettings` and on the input, so that the page can show and, with a typed confirmation, switch it. Proof: `RetentionSection.test.tsx`. D118.

| | Threat | Mitigation | Proof | S# |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T | A pull request address from the server is a `javascript:` or `data:` URL, or an address with credentials, and becomes a link. | `prAddress` refuses it; the row or card says the server gave no address that can be opened and points to the list. | `pr-url.test.ts`, `PrJobView.test.tsx`, `PrDialog.test.tsx` | S12 |
| S | An attribution names someone the server did not: a made-up name, or a weak guess shown as a measured level. | The words are a pure function of what the server sent; a user is "Lanekeeper user" and a short id unless an Admin's user list names them; `unknown` has no score. | `components/attribution/model.test.ts`, `components/attribution/attribution.test.tsx` | S12, S25 |
| I | A retention policy deletes versions without the person understanding that it does. | The warning, the dry run for the exact values, the typed confirmation, and the statement that a deleted version cannot be restored. The job's own switch cannot be shown (see above). | `RetentionSection.test.tsx` | D118 |

### Residual risks

- **A one-time token is on the clipboard after "Copy".** Other applications and clipboard-history managers can read it and the web page cannot clear it reliably (see "Copying a one-time secret", the first row). It is the price of the human's decision of 2026-10-17; the token is shown once and revocable.
- **The editor depends on Monaco's internal HTML strings and on one `setAttribute` call.** A future version can change them, and the CSP spec will say so; until then the editor is measured only in Chromium 141 (G0) and in the pinned Chromium of CI. Firefox and Safari are not tested (W19).
- **The three patches change built-ins for the whole page while the editor is loaded.** They are narrow and measured, and every one of them can be reasoned about as "no more powerful than script that already runs", but they are an exception to "no patching of built-ins" and the lint says so by name.
- **The editor is large:** about 3.5 MB (0.9 MB gzipped) for the entry chunk, 275 KB for the worker, 153 KB for the icon font, plus the JSON mode (558 KB) when a JSON file is opened. The hub sends no `Content-Encoding`, so the transfer is the raw size unless the load balancer compresses (W28). The editor is loaded only in Edit mode.
- **Monaco's `package.json` pins `dompurify` 3.4.15, which has two low advisories (IN_PLACE), and ships its own vendored copy.** The `pnpm` override moves the package to 3.4.16; the vendored copy cannot be changed and never runs, because the entry has no Markdown-rendering contribution and the CSP spec fails if its policy is asked for.
- **The theme choice is a cookie** (`lk_theme`, not a secret) read by `/theme-init.js`, a classic script from the same origin; it is `no-store` like every file that is not under `/assets/` (W28).
- **A page that is not a Chromium-based browser is protected by the hub's CSP only** (Trusted Types is feature-detected; the app must not crash without it).
- **The e2e harness's CSRF check is a copy of the rules of `CsrfLayer`,** not the layer itself, because the hub does not compose it yet (D109 D-2). When it does, the harness proxies to it and the copy goes.
- **A resume uses the last id received, and the hub says ids are "not necessarily the order they committed"** (`crates/hub-platform/src/events/wire.rs`). An event that commits after one with a higher id, and is not delivered live, is not replayed on a resume. Events only tell the page what to refetch, so the effect is a screen that shows an old answer; a query that live events keep fresh (`EVENT_FED`, never stale by age) stays old until the next event that names it, a resync, or its entry being dropped. The queries nothing names (the audit trail, admin lists, the user) are `POLLED` and say so after a minute. This is a property of the log the hub keeps, and it is for the hub to close (an id that is ordered by commit, or a replay that starts a little earlier than the id it was given).
- **The first connect refetches what is on screen once** (a connection without an id is not resumed: no replay and no `resync`), so a page load asks for its first screen's data twice when the stream opens before the queries finish. The refetch is bounded and coalesced.
- **CI fetches two npm tarballs for the visual tests (W30).** The design's own runtime renders the reference pages with React 18.3.1 and asks unpkg for it; the workflow gets `react@18.3.1` and `react-dom@18.3.1` from the npm registry instead (`npm pack`), checks both tarballs against sha512 integrity values written in the workflow, and the test checks the two UMD files against the SRI in the design's `support.js`. They are test inputs, never bundled and not dependencies of `web/`. A registry that served other bytes under those names is stopped by the pinned integrity; the unverified part is the action that uploads the baselines (`actions/upload-artifact`, pinned by SHA like the others).
- **A "Load more" can be asked for again, up to three times, after a refetch cancels it** (`src/lib/query/cursor-pages.ts`). Each ask is a bounded request with the API client's timeout; the wait for a refetch that is out is capped at the same 30 s. Nothing is retried after an error.
- **The state of a connection that the hub closes cleanly at 30 minutes is known only through the id.** If nothing with an id arrived in those 30 minutes (only heartbeats), the app refetches what is on screen after the rotation, because there is nothing to resume from.
- **A shown value can be selected or screenshotted.** A revealed value is on the screen for up to 30 seconds (less if the person hides it, switches window or tab, or opens another file); the page cannot stop a photograph, a screen recording or a selection, and it offers no copy. This is the price of letting an Editor see a flagged value at all; the reveal is audited by the server.
- **A lost reveal answer may have been recorded unseen, and a second try is a second record.** The server writes the audit record before it answers; if the answer is lost (a 500, a dropped connection, a timeout) the person cannot know whether it was written. The page says so and never retries by itself; another press is another record.
- **Markers outside the file view are drawn as text.** A diff line, a search hit, a compare hunk and a setting value that carry the marker show it as the server sent it (`[REDACTED: N chars, rule]`); only the file view (and the served text and docs, which share its renderer) draws a "Hidden value" token. The marker holds no part of the value, so this is a matter of reading, not of disclosure.
- **The retention job's own dry-run switch is not shown** (see "Pull requests, attribution, severity and the admin sections of T5"): a person cannot tell from the page whether the nightly job deletes. Proposed contract follow-up: `job_dry_run`.
- **An OS Login entry whose name contains a slash cannot be deleted from the page** (the API client refuses an encoded slash). The page does not create one; one that the hub supplies would have to be removed another way.
- **Names of people are known only to an Admin.** A Viewer, an Operator or an Editor sees "Lanekeeper user" and the start of an id, because the only list of users is Admin-only (CG14).
- **None (T6):** pickup chips, notify and "As served" are not built yet, and their rows are added with them.

## Known residual risks (from design)

- **Partly attributed NFS changes.** With the sentinel (D72), edits made on the VM are attributed to a named OS Login user. Writes from NFS clients that happen outside a sync Job are attributed only to "an NFS client", at medium confidence. A root user on the VM could stop auditd; the sentinel's heartbeat gaps are flagged as findings.
- **Cursor's model providers.** Config content sent through Cursor reaches Cursor's model providers. This is accepted because the files contain no secrets (D18), and the six flagged files are pending review (Q20).
- **Delayed lockout.** A user disabled in Entra keeps any active session until it is ended in the app or expires, which takes at most 12 hours. Mitigation: admins can disable the user in the app, which ends their sessions immediately.
